Rob Lowe Makes Rare Comment About His Sex Tape Scandal

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Decades after Rob Lowe's sex tape with a woman and a teenage girl—whom he said he believed was at least 21—was leaked, he reflected on the scandal in his new doc Rob Lowe: The Outsider.

Rob Lowe is looking back at a difficult chapter.

Nearly 40 years after the Parks and Rec actor was at the center of a sex tape scandal involving a then 22-year-old woman and a 16-year-old girl, he shared insight into the devastating toll it took on him.

"I'm not trying to do revisionist history, but we were in an over-21 bar," Lowe recalled in his new Netflix documentary Rob Lowe: The Outsider. "I'm a known film actor, and I had to show my ID to get in. I had no idea that one of the girls was underage."

The St. Elmo's Fire star, then 24—who eventually settled a lawsuit with the teen's family out of court and was not charged with a crime—knew that there was always a possibility that filming his encounter with the women could "get out." (Though the age of consent in Georgia, where the encounter took place, is 16, filming a person under 18 engaging in sexual activity is a federal crime.)

"People would ask me to pay a lot of money to try to keep the tape quiet, and I was unwilling to be blackmailed," he shared before sardonically asking, "Boy, I showed them, didn't I?"

When the video did leak in 1989, one year after it was filmed, Lowe learned he was "leading the news" while attending Cannes Film Festival to announce his 1990 film Bad Influence.

"I was advised by Hollywood's finest to not defend myself, so I didn't because they're the experts," he said of the sex tape's aftermath. "As everything began to explode around me, I just hunkered down. Like I stayed in my house. I didn't leave my house."

Since the scandal coincided with the promotion of his movie—which also starred James Spader and Marcia Cross—Lowe faced a barrage of questions about the incident.

"You'd go promote the movie, and you'd get an entire interview and then they go, 'Let me just, one thing I wanna ask you,'" he explained. "I just kind of soldiered on."

As the West Wing star struggled in the aftermath—which included an ill-fated Oscars opening ceremony performance—it was hosting Saturday Night Live in 1990 that ultimately changed the trajectory of his life. Now, he was laughing with the public, rather than just being laughed at.

"Whenever a public figure gets in trouble, Lorne Michaels comes a-callin,'" he explained. "They called me right after the video broke and wanted me to come on and do a bit. I said, 'Call me back when you want me to host.'"

And soon, Lowe found himself at Rockefeller Center's famed Studio 8H alongside the likes of Mike Myers, Dana Carvey and Nora Dunn.

"I was willing to stand in the fire and take my slings for a good laugh, and it wasn't meant as a rehabilitation," he clarified. "To me, it was really, I got a chance to host Saturday Night Live, which is something I'd been wanting to do forever."

Though Lowe—who shares sons Matthew Lowe, 33, and John Owen Lowe, 31, with wife Sheryl Berkoff—has expressed immense regret about the sex tape over the years, he previously revealed that the incident helped him reprioritize his life.

"It's not a great recipe for success to give an 18-year-old male fame, money, drugs," the actor—who got sober in 1990 after a years-long struggle with alcohol and cocaine—said on SiriusXM in 2019, "and expect there not to be something that goes wrong."

"It got me sober, sober got me married," he added. "I've been married 29 years and I have two great sons. I don't think any of that happens without going through that scandal."

For a closer look at more celebrities who've shared insight into their own sobriety journeys, keep reading.

After the two-time NBA champ was arrested and charged with a DUI in January 2026, he became informed on how to fight against his addictions.

“If you do your education, addiction [is where] you suffer from a brain disease, just like cancer or any other disease," he said during an appearance on Page Six Radio that April. "I don't think anyone would wish to have cancer.”

Lamar added, “So, me being educated on disease, the more and more I get educated, the more and more it's easier for me to stay sober.”

The basketball star—who survived a near-fatal drug overdose in 2015—had previously detailed the inspiration he hopes to be to others after passing 60 days of being "completely sober."

“I have a certain type of mental and physical endurance, but when it comes to getting high and escaping, I'm just like everyone else,” he told USA Today in March 2026. “If I could just help the average person and give them faith and hope that they can overcome it, then that's probably one of the reasons why the Lord spared me.”

In October 2018, the American Pie star announced he was one year sober in a celebratory and encouraging Instagram post.

“I first tried to get sober over 5 years ago, when the weight of my obsession with booze and drugs became too heavy for me to handle,” he wrote at the time. “Turns out this s--t is hard.”

He continued, “After some fits and starts, I’ve managed to put together one year of sobriety. I’m as proud of it as anything in my life. If you’re struggling, know there’s help. Don’t be ashamed. We can do this.”

Years later, he reflected on his journey with addiction and the lengths he went to hide the his struggle.

“I’m going to therapy and ‘working on things’ but meanwhile I’m leaving therapy, having just had a good session, and I’m going to the liquor store and buying a fifth of vodka, drinking it and then driving home,” he said on a March 2024 episode of his wife Jenny Mollen's All the Fails podcast. “I knew how to get wasted enough to where I took myself out of the life equation, took myself out of the present, didn’t have to connect in a way that made me feel things.”

He added, “I had it figured out to a T. To not get too drunk where I couldn’t have a conversation with you. I was replacing those bottles in the bar all the time.”

The two-time FIFA Women's World Cup champ has been open about her misuse of Adderall while battling injuries during her college soccer career.

“I was getting really heavily addicted to Adderall and misusing it,” she said on a 2025 episode of the Question Everything with Danielle Robay podcast. “I was taking it all the time. I would go days without sleeping. It was wild. I felt like I was gonna give myself a heart attack. I was crushing it. I was snorting it. It was so problematic.”

However, in the midst of her struggle, her college coach Anson Dorrance was there to support her.

“It got really, really dark and I had to make some serious adjustments,” she explained, “and Anson would have me read books. I started reading this book called [Man’s Search] for Meaning and it's about suffering and reframing it and it changed my life.”

In October 2025, the Migos member shared he was four years sober from codeine—an opioid used to make the soda-based drink sizzurp—after a "wake-up call" from his teenage son Jordan.

"My oldest son kept asking for like pineapple Fanta, but he don't even really drink soda," Offset recounted on Baby, This Is Keke Palmer. "And then one day he came in studio like, ‘Why yours is different color than mine?’ It killed me."

Explaining how the innocuous remark "touched" a nerve, Offset continued, "When I went in my room, I damn near cried."

"I felt bad as a father," the rapper added. "He don't even notice it’s drugs in here though. He don't know, but in my head it's like, him even just saying he want the same soda I want. It's just like, ‘Oh no, I'm influencing him.'"

Over the years, the two-time Oscar winner has spoken candidly about December 29, 1975: the day he got sober.

“I was drunk and driving my car here in California in a blackout,” he revealed in October 2025 on the New York Times podcast The Interview podcast, “no clue where I was going, when I realized that I could have killed somebody—or myself, which I didn’t care about—and I realized that I was an alcoholic.”

After reaching out to a friend for help, he was able to begin a new path. And in December 2025, he reached 50 years of sobriety, doing so with a message to those in a similar position he found himself in a half century ago.

“Choose life instead of the opposite,” he said in an Instagram message. “Life life life and more life.”

The Valley star shared his decades-long battle with drugs in March 2025 after going to rehab.

"I am coming out that I'm an addict," Jax shared during the March 4 episode of Bravo's Hot Mic podcast. "I have substance issues, primarily with cocaine. It’s hard to say out loud."

"I’ve been dealing with this on and off since I was 23 and now I’m 45," he continued. "There was times where I would stop doing it but then there were times where I’d go heavy on it.”

Jax also gave up drinking, explaining, "I don’t necessarily think I have an alcohol problem, but the two go hand in hand. I can’t do cocaine without drinking. So, I just gave up both. I’m proud to say I’m 82, 83 days sober right now, which is the longest I’ve ever gone in my life without either.”

The Spider-Man: No Way Home star stopped drinking alcohol in 2022 after feeling "enslaved" to the drink. "I was definitely addicted to alcohol, not shying away from that at all," Holland recalled on the On Purpose with Jay Shetty podcast. "I think that anyone that has a beer everyday has probably got a little bit of a problem."

"And then you would just reach that moment where you're like 'Wow, I shouldn't have had that last beer,'" the actor continued. "And you wake up the next day and you have a terrible headache."

Since making the lifestyle change, Holland noticed he "could sleep better" and "handle problems better."

"Things that would go wrong on set that would normally set me off, I could take in my stride," he shared. "I had so much such better mental clarity. I felt healthier. I felt fitter."

The star of Hunt for Red October does not often speak to his sobriety journey, which began almost forty years ago.

"I don't discuss this a lot," he said during a May 2024 podcast appearance. "I discuss it every now and then when it makes sense. I'm 39 years sober. I got sober Feb. 23, 1985."

Speaking about his life after moving from New York City to Los Angeles in 1983, Alec explained, "I had a white-hot problem every day for two years. I think I snorted a line of cocaine from here to Saturn. We took it back home. I mean, cocaine was like coffee back then. Everybody was doing it all day long."

And after quitting the drug, he soon turned to alcohol—only to also quit that dependency.

"New York relaxes me," he shared of his home. "I walk around and I see aspects of it that I've never seen before. I'll look at a building and I'll go, 'My God, I never noticed that about that building, those doors.' I have lunches and coffee and my friends."

The Princess Diaries alum shared in April 2024 that she is more than five years sober. As she told the New York Times, "That feels like a milestone to me."

Her reasoning was clear. "I knew deep down it wasn't for me," she shared. "And it just felt so extreme to have to say, 'But none?' But none. If you're allergic to something or have an anaphylactic reaction to something, you don't argue with it. So I stopped arguing with it."

And she's happy she made that decision. "My personal experience with it is that everything is better," she continued. "For me, it was wallowing fuel. And I don't like to wallow."

While accepting the 2018 Woman of the Year Award by the Peggy Albrecht Friendly House, a residential program for women recovering from substance and alcohol abuse, Moore spoke about the "self-destructive path" that nearly derailed her career.

"I feel like there's a defining moments in our lives that shape who we are and the direction we go and early in my career, I was spiraling down a path of real self-destruction and no matter what successes I had, I just never felt good enough," the star recalled. "I had absolutely no value for myself and this self-destructive path, it very quickly brought me to a real crisis point and it wasn't clear at the time the reason. Maybe it was divine intervention."

Moore, who sought treatment in 2012, thanked two unnamed people she "barely knew" for delivering something of an ultimatum to her to turn her life around. "Unless I was dead, I better show up," she quipped, adding that she was given "a chance to redirect the course of my life before I destroyed everything."

"Clearly they saw more of me than I saw of myself," Moore noted. "And I'm so grateful because without that opportunity, without their believing me, I wouldn't be standing here today."

Cyrus shared in 2022 that she's been in recovery for her Xanax addiction since 2020. "It gave me so much structure in the time that I really needed structure, because I didn't want to just be sitting around and stirring in my brain," she told Rolling Stone. "It gave me hope."

The singer added, "Once I felt that it was possible to silence things out for a second and numb your pain, it was over."

She said her friends at the time "kind of cosigned" her drug use and "it just kind of becomes this dark pit, bottomless pit." But, during the pandemic, Cyrus said she was noticing challenges and felt she wasn't emotionally present. "I was completely nodding off and falling asleep," she recalled. "And unable to keep my head up or keep my eyes open, because I was so far gone."

She eventually sought help and noted that "it took some time to get on my own two feet."

The "Lose Yourself" rapper reflected on his prior prescription pill abuse in his 2025 documentary Stans, noting an overdose and missing his daughter Hailie Jade's birthday party prompted a change.

“I cried because it was like, ‘Oh my god, I missed that,’” he said in the doc. “I kept saying to myself, ‘Do you want to f--king miss this again? Do you want to miss everything? If you can’t do it for yourself, you f--king pussy, at least do it for them.’”

Eventually, channeling his energy back into his music helped the Oscar winner on his sobriety journey.

“It did something. It turned the light on,” he explained. “I realized I’m not embarrassed anymore about [sobriety]. I started treating sobriety like a super power and I took pride in the fact that I was able to quit.”

The Parenthood alum was sober for 16 years before relapsing in 2020 by taking Vicodin after a motorcycle accident.

"For the last eight weeks maybe, I don't really know…I'm on them all day," he said on his Armchair Expert podcast. "And I'm allowed to be on them at some dosage because I have a prescription and then I'm also augmenting that."

"And I hate it," he continued. "And I'm lying to other people. And I know I have to quit. But my tolerance is going up so quickly that I'm now in a situation where I'm taking, you know, eight 30s a day, and I know that's an amount that's going to result in a pretty bad withdrawal. And I start getting really scared, and I'm starting to feel really lonely. And I just have this enormous secret."

In 2018, the Halloween star got very candid about her 10-year addiction to opiates, which began in the late '80s after a minor plastic surgery "for my hereditary puffy eyes." The reslt was a prescription that changed her life. "I had a 10-year run, stealing, conniving," she told People. "No one knew. No one."

When her sister visited her in the summer of 1998 and brought along prescribed painkillers for a rib injury, Curtis said she hit her rock bottom. "I knew she had them in her suitcase in our guest room closet," she told the publication, crying at the memory. "I basically took all her opiates. When she was leaving I knew she would pack her suitcase and find her pills missing. I knew I had to acknowledge to her what I had done, and so I wrote her a note and left it on her suitcase. I came home that day, and she put her arms around me and told me she loved me and she was concerned about me and she was unwilling to watch me kill myself."

She attended her first meeting on Feb. 3, 1999, the same day she revealed her struggle to her filmmaker husband Christopher Guest. "He was incredulous that he'd never noticed," Curtis said, adding that she's been sober ever since.

Barrymore, who previously went to rehab for alcohol and drug addiction, shared in 2021 that she was marking a big milestone in her journey. "I'm just going to say something for the first time in a long time: I have not had a drink of alcohol in two and a half years," she said on CBS Mornings. "And it was something that I realized just did not serve me in my life."

She added, "It's so funny. When we reveal ourselves and our truths and the things we've worked so hard for, it's so liberating and vulnerable all at the same time."

Barrymore later explained to the Los Angeles Times that she doesn't use the "sober" label because she doesn't want people to think she's "some perfect Puritan."

"I kept thinking, ‘I'll master this. I'll figure it out,'" she said in 2023. "And finally, I just realized: ‘You've never mastered this, and you never will.'"

The A Star Is Born actor struggled with a cocaine addiction during the early aughts before friend Will Arnett stepped in and confronted him out about his behavior.

"That was the first time I ever realized I had a problem with drugs and alcohol⁠," Cooper recalled during a 2022 appearance on the Smartless podcast. "I'll just never forget it."

Crediting Arnett as "the reason" he went sober, Cooper added, "He took that risk of having a hard conversation with me that put me on a path of deciding to change my life."

The Fight Club star spent years struggling with alcohol before Cooper helped him get sober. "I got sober because of this guy," he told the audience at the 2020 National Board of Review Annual Awards after Cooper presented him with a trophy. "And every day's been happier ever since."

In September 2023, the Cruel Intentions alum shared on Instagram that this was "the longest I've gone since I was a teen without some kind of nicotine or marijuana in my system (among other things)."

"Feeling thankful for the freedom that comes with breaking addictions and dependency on substance," he added. "Sobriety, clarity, and spiritual connectedness feels real good."

After publicly celebrating six years of sobriety on tour in 2018, the singer stunned fans later that same year with the release of the confessional single "Sober," which revealed that she had relapsed.

"Mama, I'm so sorry I'm not sober anymore / And Daddy please forgive me for the drinks spilled on the floor / To the ones who never left me / We've been down this road before I'm so sorry, I'm not sober anymore," she sang.

A month later, Lovato was hospitalized for a near-fatal overdose.

"I have always been transparent about my journey with addiction," she wrote on Instagram two weeks later. "What I've learned is that this illness is not something that disappears or fades with time. It is something I must continue to overcome and have not done yet...I now need time to heal and focus on my sobriety and road to recovery. The love you have all shown me will never be forgotten and I look forward to the day where I can say I came out on the other side. I will keep fighting."

In 2018, she recalled battling her addictions to sex and alcohol. "My sort of addictions jump. They jump around. When I was younger, I definitely think I had a sex addiction of some kind, yes, that everything could be fixed by sex," she said, before sharing about the time she realized she'd developed a troubling drinking problem.

"I remember reaching a rock bottom that time I was in the house by myself and I had those two bottles of wine and was going for the third bottle," she said. "And I was like, ‘Now hold up. You're in the this house by yourself going onto your third bottle of wine? You might have a problem.'"

"So I went cold turkey. That's the thing about me: I can go cold turkey. I am a binger, and I always have to watch myself and I can just get obsessed with things," she continued. "It's not what you're doing but how you're with it. Why you're doing it. It's the behavior that's attached to it because if you want to have a lot of sex, that's great, but why are you having all that sex? That's what you've got to look at."

The Backstreet Boys member tried drugs for the first time before filming the music video for 2000's "The Call," telling Good Morning America years later that he "was off the walls" during the shoot. He got sober in 2021 following a relapse and saw numerous positive benefits just one year later, including losing 32 pounds in seven months by cutting out alcohol and fast food.

"I'm sticking this one out all the way,” he told People in July 2025. "It's a daily thing. But I have finally done what has been suggested amongst the sober community, and my life has flipped in a positive way."

While AJ acknowledged he is "still learning, still growing," he's now confident in who he is.

"I've discovered new verbiage, such as boundaries, never really had those," he continued. "Now I have those, which is a blessing. AJ is a member of a group. He is a persona, but he doesn't define me as an individual. Alexander James is who I am."

After the sudden death of Mac Miller, the rapper—born Shad Moss—took to Twitter to let his fans know that he'd once struggled with a cough syrup addiction. "To the youth- Stop with these dumb ass drugs," he began his series of tweets. "Im going to let something out. When me and Omarion worked on FACE OFF album. I was high off lean everyday! When yall saw me on BET going off on Torae i was high off lean. My attitude everything changed. My fans started to...Turn on me my family too. I never promoted lean in my songs. The whole time i was on the UCP tour with Chris [Brown] I WAS SIPPING 4's atleast 7 times a day. I was addicted until our show in Cincinnati.. i came off stg and passed out woke up in the hospital i was having withdraws.""I never felt a pain like that ever," Bow Wow continued. "It was summer but i was walking round with 3 hoodies on because i was so cold. I missed the chicago show of that tour baltimore show BECAUSE I WAS F--KING HIGH AND SICK!!!! that s--t is not cool and i was doing it to be cool! Kick that s--t! Be a good son or daughter. Be the best you. Ima start being more vocal. We gotta save the youth from going out early. Parents watch your kids. Explain to them. We want yall to live man. I almost died f--king with syrup.""To this day im affected my stomach will...Never be the same and it hasnt been. DRUG FREE IS THE WAY TO BE! smarten up tighten up out here," he told his fans. "We cant lose no more of you. Not one! I love all yall. The young artist all the kids around the world.. dont follow a trend. Break the cycle. PEACE."

With the dual releases of her fourth studio album No Shame and memoir My Thoughts Exactly, the singer spent much of 2018 getting brutally honest about the height of her addition problems. According to Allen, she was abusing cocaine and drinking to the point of being "parisitically drunk. Ultimately, Gwyneth Paltrow and Chris Martin staged an intervention after she accidentally headbutted Orlando Bloom at Kate Hudson's Halloween party and knocked herself out.

She told The Guardian, "Nothing seemed to reach or satisfy me. I remember waking up one morning in those dark days thinking, ‘Maybe it's time for heroin, because nothing else is working.'"

The Parent Trap actor battled a cocaine addiction throughout the '80s that sent him to rehab in 1990. As he later explained on Today, he grew up in the '60s and '70s when "there was a completely different attitude" towards the drug.

"It was even in some movie budgets. I was basically doing cocaine pretty much on a daily basis during the '80s," he admitted in 2018. "I spent many, many a night screaming at God to 'Please take this away from me and I'll never do it again, cause I've only got an hour before I have to be at work.' Then at 4 o'clock in the afternoon I'd go, 'Oh it's not so bad.'"

"I had what I call a white light experience where I saw myself either dead or losing everything that meant anything to me," he continued. He had to sit down then-fiancée Meg Ryan and explain what was going on. Then, he sought help. "That was the end of the love affair with me and cocaine."

After an incident in Newport Beach where the Olympic swimmer tried to kick in his own hotel room door, Lochte made the decision to seek treatment in 2018. "Ryan has been battling from alcohol addiction for many years and unfortunately it has become a destructive pattern for him," his rep told E! News in a statement. "He has acknowledged that he needs professional assistance to overcome his problem and will be getting help immediately."

"Ryan knows that conquering this disease now is a must for him to avoid making future poor decisions, to be the best husband and father he can be, and if he wants to achieve his goal to return to dominance in the pool in his 5th Olympics in Tokyo in 2020," the statement concluded.

Although the Parenthood alum knows that "one of the things that you're not supposed to do is get sober for somebody else," his relationship with now-wife Melanie Lynskey was a major reason that led him to swearing off alcohol.

"At a point, I knew how amazing she was and I thought she would be incredible for someone who deserved her," he explained during a March 2023 appearance on The Drew Barrymore Show, "and I didn't feel like I was that person. I felt a little bit too crazy."

However, as Ritter noted to E! News, he now sees that sobriety is ultimately a personal choice.

"It was easier for me to say that I'm doing it for her because, at that time, I didn't feel like I was sort of worth much," he recalled of the early days of their relationship. "I didn't care as much about hurting myself as I did about hurting her. So, initially it was easier for me to say, 'I'm doing this for her.' And now, I'm doing it for myself."

The country star has been sober for nearly a decade, but it was a hard-fought road getting there. His addiction to alcohol and pain pills began soon after graduating from high school and didn't abate when his career began to take off. He kept a black leather laptop bag filled with bottles of Jagermeister or vodka "at arm's reach at all times," he told previously revealed to People, adding that throughout the day, "every hour and a half to two hours it'd be time to get two or three good pulls on the bottle. And every three or four hours it's time for a pill or two."

And it had no effect on his success. "It wasn't like I was stumbling around all day," he admitted. "I was fully functioning — I wrote more songs then than I do now. That was the scary part."

In late 2011, he was hospitalized with pancreatitis and was told that if he didn't stop drinking, he wouldn't see his next birthday. "I still put it off and was trying to slow down on my own, like, 'All right I'm only gonna let myself take two pills today. I'm only gonna drink this much of my bottle and make a mark on the bottle,'" he recalled. "And it would work a couple days—and then somebody throws a party."

He eventually sought help, entering rehab on Dec. 18, 2011. "I got to the point where I knew it was something I couldn't do on my own," Gilbert said. "Pissed me off to no end and embarrassed me. I'm a pretty strong-willed person but that was the one thing in my life that I couldn't get to stick."

Juventus captain Locatelli opens up on ‘one of the most difficult moments of my career’

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Manuel Locatelli has admitted he is going through one of the hardest periods of his career as he recovers from a serious knee injury.

Speaking to TuttoJuve, with quotes via CalcioMercato, the Juventus captain was answering questions from young supporters in the club’s traditional Junior Report feature.

Locatelli has been out since the 1-1 draw with Milan on 6 September. He suffered a sprain to his right knee and underwent surgery to repair the lateral meniscus, and he isn’t expected back in full training before March 2027.

“This is definitely one of the most difficult moments, because I hurt my knee and I have to stay away from the pitch for a long time, so I’m living life in a very different way,” Locatelli said.

“Another difficult moment was when we didn’t manage to go to the World Cup. When you lose, and lose like that, it’s a moment of suffering. But in the end, in football you win and you lose. The important thing is to improve and give everything you’ve got.”

The midfielder insisted his rehabilitation is going well.

“I feel good, in the sense that I’m working well. I know I have to wait, and unfortunately that sometimes happens in our lives as athletes,” he said. “I’m approaching each day differently and doing more work in the gym. I miss playing football so much, but I’ve learned to accept this situation, and I know I have to do my best to come back well.”

Locatelli joined from Sassuolo in 2021 and has since taken on the armband. He credited the leaders who came before him.

“I was lucky when I arrived at Juventus to have great captains ahead of me. Chiellini was still here, and Bonucci, then I had Danilo. It was very important for me to learn their attitude from them, always being present and available,” he said.

“I think the secret is dedication, the desire to work and improve, but above all being willing to listen to others.”

Locatelli also remembered his own childhood heroes.

“I was already a Juventus fan as a child, so I had Nedvěd, Buffon and Del Piero. Then of course I admired Totti too. They were my idols, the players I grew up with,” he said.

“Sometimes children see us that way, and that gives us a responsibility. We need to be an example, not just in our technical play during the match but in our attitude on and off the pitch. It’s a beautiful and important responsibility.”

His absence has been a blow for Luciano Spalletti, who is also without Kenan Yildiz and Khéphren Thuram.

Resurfaced clip reveals Michael Jordan link that changed Kobe Bryant’s entire career

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Kobe Bryant's career trajectory changed because of Michael Jordan.

In 1996, there were reportedly rumors that the Chicago Bulls superstar was going to retire and Bryant didn't want to pass up a chance to play against him.

Lower Merion's Kobe Bryant goes to the net at the Palestra in a Feb 27, 1996 playoff game against Coatsville. MediaNews Group via Getty Images

In a clip from a 2024 appearance on Byron Scott's "Fast Break" podcast, former NBA player John Salley recalled Bryant explaining why he skipped college and entered the 1996 NBA Draft directly out of high school.

"I said to him, 'Why'd you not go to college?' And he says, 'I heard MJ was going to retire,'" Salley said. "And that was his reason for not going to college."

Bryant was selected 13th overall in the 1996 NBA Draft by the Charlotte Hornets and was immediately traded to the Los Angeles Lakers.

Los Angeles Lakers guard Kobe Bryant(L) and Chicago Bulls guard Michael Jordan(R) talk during a free-throw attempt during the fourth quarter 17 December at the United Center in Chicago. AFP/Getty Images

Lakers #24 Kobe Bryant pumps his fist during the fourth quarter. Anthony J. Causi

Jordan ultimately continued playing for Chicago through 1998. After retiring for a second time, he returned with the Washington Wizards in 2001 and permanently ended his career in 2003.

Bryant and Jordan faced each other eight times during the regular season between 1996 and 2003. They never met in the playoffs or NBA Finals, but their matchups helped connect two of basketball's most celebrated careers.

5 Strategies From ‘Bear Brawl’ to Help You Train for the Impossible

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Sign up below to receive our newest workout routines, recipes, news stories, and offers from our partners

With the right plan and the right discipline, you can get seriously shredded in just 28 days.

At age 62, "Big Bill" shares his wisdom to dominate one of the ultimate strength marks.

Follow these fit women we're crushing on for inspiration, workout ideas, and motivation.

Revenge may be a dish best served cold, but when your target is a giant black bear, you’d better bring a perfect plan—or you may end up as the appetizer.

That’s the premise of Jon Finkel’s latest novel, Bear Brawl, an outrageously well-thought-out mix of modern training methodology soaked with an over-the-top ’80s action movie scenario that every Stallone and Schwarzenegger film fan has wanted answered: Is it possible to plan a fight against a rogue, 450-pound bear—the same unrepentant ursid that killed Brick Bridger’s mother and left his brother in a wheelchair—and win in a hand-to-hand duel?

As an ’80s expert who speaks fluent Roadhouse, I was able to read Bear Brawl in one sitting—something I’d never done with a book before—and that’s exactly the experience Finkel was aiming for. It’s also why the book would absolutely make a solid stocking stuffer this Christmas. Picture Bear Brawl as Rambo, John Matrix, and Dalton, all trained by Mr. Miyagi.

That brings us to Brick Bridger, a beast in his own right. He’s an Arkansas-bred 6’6”, 265-pound former UFC heavyweight and two-time collegiate wrestling champion who wasn’t around the night “Macho,” the quarter-ton Colossus, killed his mother and crippled his brother. He’s been living with that guilt ever since, convinced none of it would have happened had he been there.

“He’s the perfect human specimen,” Finkel said. “Brick’s a two-time NCAA wrestling champion, with eight fights in the UFC heavyweight division before retiring because of injury. And a rogue black bear—the one night he’s late to a barbecue—kills his mom and puts his brother in a wheelchair. So now we have the man, we have the means, we have the motivation.”

Instead of spiraling, he uses his depression as fuel to take on Macho. Only with the help of several experts and a commitment to a plan does the idea of fighting the bear seem, well… still ridiculous. But somewhat more attainable than going into the woods carrying nothing but rage.

For Finkel, an M&F contributing writer and editor who witnessed fight camps with UFC legends such as Brock Lesnar, Randy Couture, and Frank Mir, the novel is more than a tribute to the ’80s action adventure genre. There’s also a bit of strategy readers can incorporate into their own goal-setting, without actually having to grapple a grizzly.

“Turn your grief or stress into a healthy challenge,” he said. “A lot of people, they’ll start triathlons. They’ll do Ironmans to get over something, to occupy their mind. In this case, it’s not an Ironman or a marathon. It’s to fight a bear.”

Whether it’s losing weight, running a marathon or staying true to a 12-week training program for a BJJ tournament, each of these will have its own set of obstacles that may appear overwhelming at certain points. Like Bridger, with the right help and strategy, even the most ridiculous of challenges can become attainable—on paper at least.

1. Choose a Goal that Gives Your Training Direction

A clear target can turn scattered and inconsistent workouts into preparation with purpose. Whatever your type of activity you’re into, select an event or milestone that excites you, then break it into manageable steps. Study its requirements, plan your training, then execute and track your progress.

“You take a task and whether it’s impossible or not—or you think it’s impossible—and you train all your energies toward it,” Finkel says. “Yours may be completing an Ironman or marathon. Brick’s was fighting a bear.”

2. Find the Obstacle—and Make a Plan for It

No matter what your skill level is, there will come a point in which your progress stalls. When it happens, don’t assume that the solution is simply to work harder. Take inventory of what may be the cause of your plateau. Is it a physical weakness? Technique? Consistency? Recovery? Brick understood the challenge and searched for an opening. For your fitness goals, it could mean hiring a coach, making program adjustments, or even giving yourself more time to build your skillset.

“You got to know your enemy, and you have to be humble,” Finkel says. “He looks until he eventually finds the Achilles’ heel. Maybe there is always a way.”

3. Work on Weak Points While Still Enhancing Your Strengths

A smart plan is a combination of making improvements while also capitalizing on your primary abilities. Brick used his own size and MMA skills as the foundation while also putting in the work in the “classroom” to search for new ways to victory. For you, build on your strengths, too, from choosing a training style that fits your abilities, then working and progressing on the secondary skills that will help get the job done.

“Lean into your strength,” Finkel says. “Brick’s not an in-his-prime Mike Tyson knocking out guys in the first round. Every fight is now a grind for him. I even did this myself—I was trying to do triathlons, and I realized the only thing I actually like is swimming. So I just became a really fast swimmer and forgot the two parts I hated. As soon as I leaned into the thing I was best at, I enjoyed it so much more. So pick one and be really good at it.”

A go-it-alone strategy heavily reliant on brute strength and past personal achievements ends in disaster for nearly all of us. For Brick, filling the gaps for this task meant incorporating experts who knew the right tendencies and could spot openings and weak spots the untrained eye would overlook. So when it comes to goal achievement, it helps to find a coach, training partner, or knowledgeable source who can add to your preparations, spot problems, and offer candid feedback.

“Brick had a zoologist and a wrestling coach explaining the ways it can’t work,” Finkel says. “Being creative is one thing, but being resourceful within that creativity can really make a project like this work.”

An outrageous goal will take longer than you expect, and there will be stretches where it looks like you’re losing. Brick’s MMA motor keeps him moving forward when every fight becomes a struggle. He keeps working his way toward that small but decisive opening he knows is there but isn’t quite visible yet.

“He doesn’t quit,” Finkel says. “Brick might not be the most technically sound guy. He might not even be the strongest. He’s the guy with the motor, the size, and the will to keep figuring things out until he can exploit the weakness. Most people’s goals die right at the point where it stops feeling good—keep grinding until it does.”

PSG’s Champions League ‘obsession’ cost them league title under Tuchel, says Florenzi

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Former Paris Saint-Germain defender Alessandro Florenzi believes their "obsession" with winning the Champions League cost them the Ligue 1 title under Thomas Tuchel.

Florenzi spent the 2020-21 season on loan at PSG, who lost in the Champions League final to Bayern Munich in the previous campaign.

Tuchel won 74.8% of his games in charge of PSG (G127 W95 D13 L19), his best win percentage at any club, and led them to back-to-back titles in 2018-19 and 2019-20, but things unravelled the following season.

PSG sacked Tuchel in December 2020 after they were falling behind in the Ligue 1 title race to Lille, who would go on to win the league by one point.

It was only the second season since 2012-13 in which PSG failed to win the league title, after Monaco won the French top-flight in 2016-17.

However, Tuchel was hired by Chelsea a month after being sacked by PSG and went on to win the Champions League with the Blues in that same season.

Florenzi described Tuchel's Champions League win as "karma", but the Italian was not surprised to see PSG eventually win the competition.

"At the time, PSG hadn't won the Champions League yet, but you could see the president wanted to build something big," Florenzi told L'Equipe.

Champions League eve at the PSG Campus!  #PSGRoma I #UCL https://t.co/qRAAgpBlQh

— Paris Saint-Germain (@PSG_English) November 24, 2026

"Their obsession was the Champions League. And that's undoubtedly what cost us the league title; that season, we only thought about the Champions League.

"We lost in the semi-final against Manchester City, and Tuchel won it in the final. That's karma! But I have great memories of Paris, and I won my first trophies there.

"I told myself that PSG would end up winning the Champions League, yes, but something had to happen. And that something is Luis Enrique.

"With him, it's no longer the same club."

Palace docked three points for substitution rule breach in Everton victory

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Crystal Palace have been deducted three points for breaching substitution rules during their 1-0 win over Everton on the opening day of the Women's Super League.

Lexi Lloyd-Smith scored the only goal at The 360 Engage Stadium in September, but the Eagles have since had those points removed after using too many substitution windows.

The introduction of Kirsty Howat in the 97th minute was Palace's fourth separate change, with other substitutions taking place in the 62nd, 83rd and 90th minutes.

While WSL managers are allowed to make five changes in a game, they must be made across only three stoppages in play to avoid excessive time-wasting.

An independent tribunal determined that the breach was "inadvertent" and that it was "not intended to secure an unfair advantage," but added that a sporting sanction was "necessary."

The deduction means Palace drop to 13th in the WSL and behind Birmingham City on goal difference, after the Eagles were thumped 5-1 by London City Lionesses on Sunday.

Crystal Palace have been informed of the decision by a panel to agree to the WSL’s recommendation to deduct three points from our 2026/27 Barclays Women’s Super League tally, following the 97th-minute substitution that used a ‘fourth’ window in our recent fixture against Everton. https://t.co/aNpga6nt4C

— Crystal Palace F.C. Women (@cpfc_w) November 24, 2026

The Everton match was one of only two WSL games Palace had won this season, and the Eagles said they were "disappointed" by the outcome.

"Everyone at the club is obviously frustrated that such a minor infraction, given that the player was on the pitch for only 46 seconds while the ball was in play, has led to such a significant penalty – a penalty which we do not agree is reasonable or proportionate," Palace's statement read.

"We are disappointed for the players and staff who worked so hard to secure a deserved victory on our return to the WSL."

Former Football Factory Hooligan Named England’s 5 Toughest Fanbases

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Frank Harper, who is a former Millwall hooligan and later made a name for himself as an actor, has named the five toughest fanbases in English football.

He is undoubtedly best known for playing Billy Bright, a member of the 'Chelsea Headhunters' firm in the classic 2004 hooligan film 'The Football Factory' alongside Danny Dyer. But he also played roles in 'The Rise of the Footsoldier' and 'Lock, Stock and Two Smoking Barrels'.

Harper, now 63, didn't need to do much transforming to become any of his film characters as he was a former hooligan himself with Millwall in the 70s, 80s and 90s.

In the book called Terrace Legends, via Daily Star, the Londoner picked out his top five football firms, having included two fanbases within the capital city.

While Liverpool and Manchester United have the biggest fanbases in English football thanks to their dominance in the Premier League - with the latter being crowned the toughest fanbase by another former hooligan - Harper snubbed both on his list.

In fact, there was only one northern club he ended up choosing, having named Leeds United among his five, later recalling a particular time he was involved with the Yorkshire club. He said:

"West Ham, Chelsea, Pompey (Portsmouth), Leeds and Cardiff City."

Now in his forties, Daniel Wright has completely turned his life around since winning the lottery in 2022.

He added: "Leeds at home in the league. I was in a pub and suddenly it emptied. The ones that couldn't get out the door were climbing out the toilet windows to get out on to the street.

"Thousands of Millwall came out of nowhere as bottles and glasses rained down on the Leeds lot. The Old Bill pulled a transit van across the pub doorway to try to stem the flow of Millwall fans. That day, Leeds were dead lucky to reach the ground."

Besides the five fanbases he listed, after having also named Grimsby Town - which ranks among the ugliest stadiums in British football - as the worst away ground he has ever been to, Harper was involved in another scary experience at a Millwall home game, when a pocket of Ipswich fans infiltrated the stands and after plenty of pushing, he was close to smacking his head on a metal barrier.

Musicians, actors, and other sports stars fill a complete list detailing every 92 club's msot famous supporters.

"One of my most frightening experiences at football was when Millwall played Ipswich at the Den in the '70s," he remembered. "Somehow, a mob of Ipswich came into the Cold Blow Lane End of the ground.

"Everyone pushed down the terraces towards them, and I was being carried and shoved by one mass of angry bodies. My head was heading towards a metal barrier and I just ducked underneath it, but my mate hit his head and I had to drag him up because he was nearly out cold."

Leny Yoro red card OVERTURNED: Man United star available for Brentford clash after controversial dismissal in Liverpool trouncing

📅 2026-11-24  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 10:23 EST, 24 November 2026 | Updated: 12:34 EST, 24 November 2026

Leny Yoro’s one-match ban has been lifted by the FA after Manchester United successfully appealed against his sending-off at Liverpool on Sunday, despite Pro Ref boss Howard Webb believing it was the right decision.

Yoro was shown a straight red card by referee Sam Barrott in the eighth minute for a challenge on Alexander Isak, and United’s 10 men went on to lose 4-0 in Mauricio Pochettino’s first game in charge.

The club launched an appeal over the decision and the FA have confirmed that Yoro will now be available for Saturday’s game against Brentford at Old Trafford.

A statement read: ‘Leny Yoro’s one-match suspension has been removed following a successful claim for wrongful dismissal. The Manchester United player was sent off for denying an obvious goal scoring opportunity during their Premier League match against Liverpool on Sunday 22 November. The Independent Regulatory Commission’s written reasons for the decision will be published in due course.’

Barrott sent off Yoro for pushing Isak in the back on the edge of the penalty box, and Dominik Szoboszlai scored Liverpool’s first goal from the free kick.

However, United insisted that the Frenchman did not deny Isak a clear goalscoring opportunity because goalkeeper Senne Lammens would have reached the ball first.

Leny Yoro has seen his red card suspension overturned following an appeal by Man United

The overturned decision, contradicts the opinion of Webb who backed Barrott's on-field verdict, when speaking on the Match Officials Mic'd Up show.

'Firstly, I want to say, I think this is a really decisive decision by the referee, Sam Barrott. We know it's clearly a foul. I don't think there's any dispute about that. And we also know that it's outside the penalty area. We're thinking about the sanction, aren't we that's why we're talking about this.

'Is it a denial of an obvious goal scoring opportunity? Four considerations officials have to think about. Direction of play, it's definitely going towards the goal. It's not going out wide. Proximity from goal, well, it's just outside the penalty area. The location of other defenders. Well, there's only Yoro there. The keeper is moving back towards his own goal.

'He has to put the brakes on and possibly come back out. The reason we're talking about it is the fourth consideration, that's control or likelihood of keeping on or gaining control of the ball. Isak's got control of the ball, he's got possession, he's got the touch, he knocks it past Yoro. He's moving at pace at that moment, and a foul comes in and knocks him down.

'And there's a question mark, I guess, around whether or not he would get onto that ball before Senne Lammens. Now, in the moment, the referee looked at it and felt he would.

'That was the referee's call made on the field, and then the VAR checks that to see whether that decision made on the field was clearly and obviously incorrect, and formed the opinion that it wasn't, that if there was any doubt, then it was doubt.

'It wasn't going to be something that was clearly and obviously wrong and therefore allowed that decisive on-field decision to stand. and i think by and large, most people would see that as a correct decision, with Isak moving quickly on towards that ball. The alternative view is that Lammens might get there first, but i think it's a little bit debatable, I guess. Certainly not clearly wrong.'

It’s the third time in the past 14 months that United have been on the wrong end of incorrect decisions by match officials.

Pro Ref boss Howard Webb believed it was the right decision for Yoro to be sent off on Sunday

In September, Pro Ref boss Webb apologised to United when Erling Haaland’s winning goal for Manchester City in the Old Trafford derby was allowed to stand after VAR incorrectly ruled that teammate Enzo Fernandez was not interfering with play as he tried to head the ball in an offside position.

Last season, Webb acknowledged that the match officials got it wrong when they decided not to send off Brentford captain Nathan Collins for hauling back Bryan Mbeumo inside the box as United lost 3-1 at the Gtech Community Stadium.

New United head coach Pochettino blamed Yoro’s red card for the heavy defeat in his first game in charge, and was unhappy that Barrott took no action over a similar incident involving Jeremy Jacquet and Mbeumo in the Liverpool penalty area moments earlier.

‘It's not okay to start a game in the Premier League with this situation,’ said Pochettino.

‘The game changed completely after eight minutes. Now we can explain everything, but the damage is done – it's so painful.

‘In Leny's case, it wasn't intentional. It was an unusual situation that is normal. Everyone who plays football can feel it.

‘If you review the VAR you can see for sure, he never had the intention to connect with Isak. The ball was going to the goalkeeper.

‘If you look before, in the sixth minute, Jacquet on Mbeumo. If you compare the actions, that was a penalty. We were punished badly and they were not punished, that is the truth.

‘It's clear that after eight minutes the plan was in the bin. Do you think that is easy? Even the best team in the world playing in this situation concedes four goals or maybe more.

‘The players are feeling the pain of the result. I told them to the calm: it was an unfair situation that happened.’

£3m Southampton man must seek St Mary’s exit in January after Tonda Eckert decision

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Southampton returned to winning ways with a convincing 2-0 win over Derby County on Saturday, but one selection decision made by Tonda Eckert will have one player really questioning his role in the Saints squad.

One of the main contributors to Southampton's strong start this season has been their injury bill, which has, for the most part, been impeccable.

That's left some fringe players without the minutes they'd ideally want, but on Saturday, there was a big chance for Mads Roerslev to finally stake a claim for some starts in the future, as James Bree was sidelined with a minor injury.

However, the Dane was once again left on the bench, despite being the only fit recognised right-back in Tonda Eckert's squad. Even if he still managed to get his first league minutes of the season off the bench, the writing is surely on the wall regarding the former Brentford man's status in this Saints side.

Roerslev was an intriguing signing for Southampton last season. Arriving from Brentford for £3 million, he went under the radar in a window that also saw big-money feespaid for the likes of Finn Azaz, Casper Jander, Tom Fellows and Leo Scienza.

At Brentford, though, the Dane was a useful squad player in their rise to the Premier League and the years that followed under Thomas Frank. He started every game of their successful play-off run in 2021, and in the 2023/24 campaign, he played in all but four Premier League games.

Unfortunately, Roerslev's time at Southampton so far has been largely associated with how terrible the Saints were under Will Still at the start of last season. All of the 27-year-old's starts at St Mary's came under the Belgian.

When Still was sacked in early November last year, it presented a good opportunity for the squad to impress under new, young, exciting interim coach Tonda Eckert, but Roerslev was almost immediately sidelined.

He played in Eckert's first two games, wins against QPR and Sheffield Wednesday, before going down with an Achilles injury, which would see him out for the rest of the season.

Roerslev had to watch on from the sidelines as Southampton were rampant in the second half of the season under Eckert. The Saints didn't lose a league game from January 17th until the end of the campaign, and made the FA Cup semi-finals, too.

During that time, James Bree became an unlikely hero at St Mary's, coming back from his loan spell at Charlton Athletic in January and immediately being thrown back into Saints contention under Eckert.

The 28-year-old went into this season as the first choice at right-back, but a returning Roerslev from injury made for a good backup in theory, keeping with the theme at Southampton currently that there are two top players in each position.

In practice, that hasn't been the case with Bree out, though, as the former one-time Danish international has remained on the fringes, even when the position has been open.

Against Norwich City last month, Bree was rested after playing every minute of the first nine league games of the season. In his place, though, came natural centre-back, Zach Abbott, who has also been a victim of the pecking order so far this season.

With Abbott coming in to play in the middle of the back four against Derby on Saturday and with Bree out injured, though, it gave Roerslev a big chance for his first start in over a year. But in came natural midfielder Flynn Downes, with the Dane having to settle for a nine-minute cameo.

Do you think Mads Roerslev deserves more game time at Southampton? Let us know in the comments.

Those were his first minutes of the season, but they don't indicate a resurgence in the squad.

In a way, the decisions that Tonda Eckert made regarding formation in his first few months at the helm at St Mary's have foreshadowed Roerslev's position in the pecking order right now.

Southampton flip-flopped between a back three and a back four during Will Still's uneventful few months in charge. One of the first things Eckert did was stick with a back three, but Roerslev wasn't his first choice off the right.

Indeed, in the German's first two games in charge, the two games Roerslev was fit and available for, Tom Fellows was preferred at right wing back. Saints looked to attack more down the right, and the former West Brom man was selected ahead of a natural right-back.

Then, when Bree returned from his loan spell at Charlton, that was when Eckert moved to a flat back four, which he has used almost exclusively since; the one exception being against Manchester City in the FA Cup semi-final.

Perhaps then, Roerslev's place in the pecking order was clear from the off. Eckert didn't favour him over a winger in a small sample size last season, so trusting Downes to play there against Derby may not be much of a surprise.

Football's a squad game, and Southampton may prefer to have someone with the experience of Roerslev in their reserves in case they encounter injuries during the second half of the season.

However, it's becoming apparent that the 27-year-old needs a fresh change of scenery to try and kickstart his career once more.

Which stadiums in the Championship, League One and League Two are the least spacious on the inside? FLW investigates...

Just three seasons ago, Roerslev was a regular player in a Premier League squad, making the Danish national team and not looking out of place within it. He has the quality, but isn't able to showcase it in Southampton's reserves.

Tonda Eckert has shown that he's comfortable playing central defenders or midfielders out of position at right-back, so letting him go to start afresh in January shouldn't detriment them, either.

4 Ian Holloway replacements Swindon Town must eye as sack officially confirmed

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Swindon Town are searching for a new manager with The Robins having taken the decision to sack Ian Holloway after a prolonged stretch of difficulty.

Heading into the spring last season, with League Two top scorer Aaron Drinan spearheading an often entertaining side, Swindon were right in the hunt for a top three finish and automatic promotion back to the third-tier.

They finished their campaign with just three wins in their final 13 matches to collapse from second in the table all the way down to ninth and outside of even the play-off places.

Despite that collapse, Holloway remained in situ for this season but the writing has appeared on the wall for a while now with fairly bizarre pre and post-match interviews conducted by the eccentric Bristolian and Robins' supporters becoming increasingly unhappy.

The fans’ displeasure and their expression of it was perhaps at its peak this weekend as Swindon suffered a 2-0 loss to Newport County at Rodney Parade, leaving them in the bottom half of the table.

With Holloway now out of the door at the County Ground, we have taken a look at four potential candidates who could replace the former Blackpool boss.

Swindon may well opt to look into the National League for their next manager with Craig Mahon performing well at AFC Fylde one name who could crop up but, being ambitious, Town have to try and lure Luke Garrard away from Boreham Wood.

The difficulty with that is that Garrard is currently in charge of one of the best National League teams ever at the moment – and there is some stiff competition for that given the records that have been set and broken in recent years by the likes of Barnet, Chesterfield, Notts County and Wrexham.

He has built this Boreham Wood side into the dominant force that it now is in the National League, with the North Londoners having won 15 and drawn three of their 18 matches so far, already 13 points clear of second place Harrogate Town.

Ranking the Championship clubs based on how successful they've been since the start of 2001/02

A former Swindon player, Garrard finished his playing career with Boreham Wood before taking charge as manager at the age of 30. After a nine-year stint, he left for just six months before returning in the autumn of 2024, earning their promotion back to the National League in the 2024-25 campaign before reaching the play-off final last season, coming moments away from promotion before Emmanuel Dieseruvwe’s late equaliser for Rochdale.

Now, Boreham Wood, who sold key man Abdul Abdulmalik in the summer, have taken another step forward. Garrard is going to become an EFL manager – Swindon should try and tempt him to take that step with them.

Another manager surely deserving of an opportunity to be an EFL manager, this time once again, would be former York City boss Stuart Maynard, with it being quite a surprise he has yet to be poached this far into the campaign.

Maynard led York City to the National League title in dramatic fashion last season with their incredible title race with Rochdale. However, he was surprisingly let go of by the club late on in pre-season, just as the season was about to get underway.

Maynard had previously only had one full season in the EFL, where he led Notts County to the play-off semi-finals in the 2024-25 campaign, having come in mid-way through the previous season with The Magpies a newly-promoted outfit and reeling from the departure of manager Luke Williams.

York have offered clubs in League Two a potential coup should they indeed bring Maynard in and he would appear the ideal solution to Swindon’s pronlem.

The man that Maynard replaced at Meadow Lane was Luke Williams with Williams leaving Notts County for Swansea City in the Championship. Williams’ stock has fallen a fair bit in that time but he may well still be worth the gamble for Swindon.

The football that Williams implemented at Notts County as they gained promotion from the National League with 117 goals scored in 46 matches as they finished as runners-up to Wrexham, continued into the EFL, often leading County to be regularly exposed at the back but one of the more entertaining teams with a ceiling so high that Swansea, proven to be smart recruiters over the last few years, deeming him good enough to make the step up to the second-tier.

A difficult spell in South Wales has since been followed by a tough stint in charge of Peterborough United, too, with some off-field shenanigans undermining occasional glimpses of ‘what could have been'.

‘What could have been’ at Posh may well be to the benefit of Swindon with The Robins now able to pounce and help restore the reputation of Williams with his attacking style of football perhaps well-suited to the Holloway-built squad in Wiltshire.

Another one whereby another club’s loss could be Swindon’s gain would be Andy Woodman, who has to be one of the more sought-after coaches in League Two at the moment.

Woodman has been a victim of his own success this season. Having led minnows Bromley into the EFL for the first time ever and then into League One as champions in their second season at the level, Woodman has since departed Hayes Lane this season.

Bromley, naturally, revamped their squad in the summer and looked set to be competitive over the course of a season, even with a few hammerings thrown in, but a competitive battle against the drop didn’t appear to be enough with him leaving this autumn.

Now, though, Swindon have the opportunity to bring in a manager who has been one of the great overachievers in recent EFL history and someone who was the title-winning manager in the division they want to punch their way out of just six months ago.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Mourning the untimely but inevitable loss of the season’s greatest Leeds statistic

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

It had been, for a time, one of our favourite bits of heavily caveated nonsense which straddled this and last season. It was, with one of the final kicks of an invigorating, liberating, and most importantly first of the millennium Leeds Premier League win at Stamford Bridge, obliterated in the most fitting way.

From a last-gasp Sean Longstaff equaliser against Bournemouth in April to the stoppage-time strike which crowned a 4-1 thrashing of Chelsea, Leeds scored 16 Premier League goals in as many matches.

Aaronson had played 534 minutes across those 16 games, yet somehow managed to avoid actually being on the pitch for any of those goals.

It was a run which included a couple of stints as an unused substitute, as well as full appearances or cameos in Daniel Farke’s attacking football vacuum, such as the 3-0 thrashing by West Ham in May.

But contained within it were some stunning near-misses which Aaronson must have struggled not to take entirely personally: Anton Stach’s winner against Nottingham Forest on the opening weekend, six minutes after the American came off; all four goals against Newcastle coming before his introduction, as with all three against Burnley last campaign.

The goal which kicked it all off was even laced with the sense of a specific vendetta against Aaronson: Longstaff replaced him in the 84th minute of that Bournemouth draw before snatching a point.

It was the sort of apparent individual curse which confirmed a certain bias within the Leeds fanbase. The sense that Aaronson benefits from Farke’s favouritism is not new – the Chelsea game marked his 100th appearance under the manager, a mark only six others have reached during his tenure – and the American’s presence in the team has touched a Red Bull nerve since he signed four years ago.

Aaronson, for many, is the stepchild Leeds ended up in custody of after the messy Jesse Marsch divorce.

Farke himself has taken aim at his “challenges in his offensive game” before, lamenting his “polarising” and “hectic” nature. But that usually comes as part of a ham-fisted defence of a player he feels faces “more than unfair” criticism from the supporters.

The Chelsea game was a case in point: Leeds had a commanding and deserved 3-1 lead, yet the reaction to Aaronson being the first substitute, especially in place of goalscoring hero Noah Okafor, was defined by mild frustration.

Those bristling at the sight of Aaronson receiving his tactical instructions on the touchline might ordinarily have had a point, but against this opponent in that situation, he was the best player Farke could have called on.

Set free on the counter by Harry Wilson, Aaronson made Malo Gusto and Levi Colwill look foolish before sliding an unduly composed finish through the keeper’s legs, having netted in the nine-goal Carabao Cup thriller at this stadium in September.

Adding Emi Martinez to his absurdly targeted list of Chelsea-based former The Best FIFA Men’s Goalkeeper award-winning victims alongside Edouard Mendy was a fine riposte – and end to a statistic we’ll always cherish.

Jamie Carragher sets season objective for Liverpool after eviscerating Man United at Anfield

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Jamie Carragher is hopeful that Liverpool can be the ‘strongest challenger’ to Arsenal in the Premier League title race this season after a perfect weekend for his former club.

With the Gunners and Manchester City both held to draws on Saturday, Chelsea embarrassed by Leeds, and Brighton shipping a surprise defeat to Hull on Sunday, the Reds took full advantage by destroying Manchester United 4-0 at Anfield.

Andoni Iraola’s side are fourth in the top-flight table, seven points adrift of Mikel Arteta’s league leaders but only one point behind the second-placed Seagulls, and the Sky Sports pundit wants to see the Merseysiders aim for the top two this season.

Speaking on The Gary Neville Podcast after yesterday’s emphatic win, Carragher said: “I still think there’s just an inconsistency with Liverpool game-to-game at the moment.

“I think they will get better the longer the season goes on and people want there to be a title race, but I think most people in the country would look at Arsenal as a team and say they will probably win the title comfortably.

“For me, with Liverpool being my team, I want them to be in a position to be the strongest challenger this season and put themselves in a position that they can then challenge Arsenal next season.”

Want more CaughtOffside coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

What had looked a straight shootout between Arsenal and Man City a few weeks ago now seems to be turning into a one-horse race at the summit, with Liverpool among the clutch of teams battling to be best of the rest.

Obviously seven-point gap with more than two-thirds of the season still to play can be overhauled, but the Gunners have certainly shown far more consistency than any other side in the Premier League so far this term.

City’s slump in form, Chelsea’s Jekyll-and-Hyde nature and Brighton’s sudden halt of momentum have left the door open for Iraola’s Reds to stake their claim for a top-two finish, if they can build on yesterday’s result.

Nobody at Liverpool will be getting carried away by Sunday’s events – Rio Ngumoha felt that his team could’ve scored more than four against United, and playing against 10 men for almost the full game certainly helped – but Carragher isn’t being unrealistic in calling for his former club to aim for the runners-up spot.

If the Reds’ attack can click like it did yesterday and Florian Wirtz keeps up his outstanding levels of recent weeks, the men from Merseyside could plausibly end the campaign as the closest team to Arsenal, who already appear to be in a commanding position at the head of the table.

Serie A: before challenging a diminished PSG, AS Roma crushes Parma

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

AS Roma perfectly negotiated their trip to Parma by winning largely (1-5), a few days before their European meeting against PSG in the Champions League at the Parc des Princes, during the 12th day of Serie A. Gian Piero Gasperini’s men were however shaken up at the start of the match, with several dangerous situations conceded to the locals. But Mile Svilar kept his team afloat thanks to several decisive interventions, before Donyell Malen broke the deadlock in the 39th minute. The Dutch striker perfectly exploited a serve from Lulli to score his ninth goal of the season in Serie A.

After Elphege’s equalizer after returning from the locker room (55th), the Giallorossi quickly regained control of the match. Malen scored twice in the 68th minute, before Rodrigo Mora widened the gap two minutes later on a new action initiated by Wesley. The Dutchman finally completed his performance in the 77th minute, scoring a hat-trick then a quadruplet later and taking his league tally to double figures. A convincing victory for Roma, which arrives launched before meeting PSG on Wednesday evening in the Champions League who will be deprived of Désiré Doué in addition to Khvicha Kvaratskhelia.

GdS: ‘Turbo Milan’ – how Frosinone were dismantled by five-star counter-attacking display

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

AC Milan put in one of their best performances under Ruben Amorim so far to sweep aside Frosinone at San Siro.

La Gazzetta dello Sport (seen below) begin a report by writing that Lazio, Roma and Juventus also had fun this weekend, scoring a flurry of goals in their victories, but it is Milan that might just come away from the round 12 feeling best.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Within 20 minutes, the Rossoneri had already blown the game wide open, eventually putting five past their opponents. All of them were spectacular, lightning-fast, and following the same pattern: every single one a counter-attack.

It was a throwback style of football, yet infused with the visionary approach of Amorim, making it both fresh and truly beautiful to watch. There may not be official statistics on this, but conceding from five lethal transitions feels like a record as Frosinone’s strategy certainly went beyond mere self-sabotage.

Yet, on days like this, this Milan – an enigma to its opponents, with fluid movement and interchangeable positioning – are a truly daunting prospect. When Adrien Rabiot, Diego Moreira, Luka Modrić and others perform at such a high level, we see the potential of this side.

The Diavolo have secured five wins in their last six league matches, yet in Europe, they have suffered three defeats in four games. Will Thursday’s clash against Olympiacos help restore the balance?

There was nothing balanced about the game between Milan and Frosinone. It is true that Alvini’s incredibly bold (perhaps even reckless) side tried to go toe-to-toe with their opponents for an hour by attacking, playing long balls, and taking on defenders.

Yet, beyond this emphasis on a specific style of play, one could make more concrete assessments. The coach clearly understood how vital Raimondo is to a team featuring such direct and creative wingers, Ghedjemis on the right and Kvernadze (who was rather ineffective yesterday) on the left.

Raimondo was the perfect focal point for them and without him, things had already gone poorly against Cagliari. Alvini attempted to make adjustments but got it wrong: he abandoned his 4-2-3-1 formation in favour of a back three (Calvani, Monterisi, Cittadini).

All this was undermined by a senseless tendency to push too high, maintaining a defensive line 57 metres from their own goal within a 3-4-3 setup that lacked a true striker, opting instead for a ‘false’ one in Schmid. Every time they lost possession, it resulted in a counter-attack and often a goal.

It might seem odd to start with Frosinone rather than the brilliance of Milan, but there’s no denying that the visitors played their part in the spectacle. This is a match that future opponents will need to study in minute detail, because Milan on the counter are a goal-scoring machine.

Amorim, too, has a knack for springing surprises, though as always the guessing game regarding his actual tactical setup for the day begins the moment the whistle blows. For example, it was not a back four as it appeared on paper.

Instead, the system actually gradually morphed into a 3-5-2, with Rabiot operating as a highly advanced right-sided central midfielder, almost an attacking midfielder. The key lies in the connection between the Frenchman and the stratospheric Moreira, who surged down the centre often.

Keeping everything running at full throttle was the incredible playmaking of Modrić, who also serves as the team’s primary ball-winner. So much for being a ‘pensioner’.

Milan proved flawless in exploiting both structural weaknesses and individual errors. Frosinone’s DNA is defined by a headlong, aggressive approach, but the opening goal – stemming from a sequence on the right flank involving Modrić and Rabiot – ended up as an own goal by Calvani.

Just four minutes in, and the match was effectively decided. While Frosinone usually thrive on quick, darting attacks, here the roles were reversed. Another full-pitch surge, initiated by Ramos winning the ball from Calvani and carried forward by Moreira and Rabiot, left the Portuguese player unmarked to make it 2-0.

Only a reckless back pass from Musah gifted Masini a goal to pull it back to 2-1. In the second half, however, Amorim addressed the one area that looked shaky: he withdrew Bartesaghi – who had been struggling against Ghedjemis – shifting Musah to full-back and pairing Jashari with Modrić.

It paid off immediately: Jashari broke away and fired home the 3-1 following yet another rapid transition. And that wasn’t the only one: Pulisic surged clear on his own after a Frosinone corner (4-1), and Cissé, having just come on, joined the fray at full throttle to add the fifth (5-1).

There were many top performances. Rabiot is like a freight train and deserved a goal. He had to settle for three assists (bringing his total to seven). Moreira is versatile, playing as a wing-back, a central midfielder, or a winger, much like he did for Belgium when they caused Italy so much trouble.

Ramos also moved well outside of his goal. Cissé was an absolute handful: he came on and scorrd, playing as a striker after the Portuguese felt a tweak. He did so with an effortless grace that makes him impossible for his markers to handle.

The end result is that Milan are back in fourth. The competition is fierce, but the Rossoneri are right there in the mix, and eventually, some of the others will stumble. Take Inter, for example. Frosinone need to rethink their strategy while waiting for the return of Raimondo.

They’ve overachieved so far, but now they might need to be a bit more prudent. And what about Milan? If they don’t turn their trip to Athens into a Greek tragedy, perhaps their European campaign can get back on track, too.

Liverpool told most likely Bart Verbruggen destination with Brighton planning to sell - Expert

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Liverpool are likely to lose out to Real Madrid in the race to sign Brighton goalkeeper Bart Verbruggen next summer as things stand.

That is according to former Everton chief executive Keith Wyness, speaking exclusively to Football Insider, who has a feeling that the 24-year-old will end up with the Spanish giants.

Both clubs are currently chasing Verbruggen to provide the long-term solution to their goalkeeping issues, with both Alisson and Thibaut Courtois approaching the end of their careers.

Wyness believes that Verbruggen would be an ideal fit for Liverpool, but expects them to find it very difficult to fend off Real Madrid.

He recently told Football Insider that Brighton will sell Verbruggen into an "auction" next summer, and he believes that scenario doesn't suit Liverpool.

The Dutch international has become one of the Premier League's leading goalkeepers, and his valuation next summer is likely to reflect that.

Everton’s former chief Wyness – who served as CEO at Goodison Park between 2004 and 2009 and now runs a football consultancy advising elite clubs – believes that Brighton will arguably come out as the "winners" of the situation regarding Verbruggen's future.

The boardroom expert believes that the Seagulls will be happy to let him go ahead of his contract expiring in 2028, but only at a price that suits them.

Speaking on the latest edition of Football Insider's Inside Track podcast, Wyness explained his gut instinct that Madrid is Verbruggen's most likely destination.

"Again, Brighton will come out as winners. They've done it with Chelsea, they've done it with Liverpool before; they do very well," he said.

"I think that is the case and I think they'll be happy to let him go. He's a good keeper.

"You're right with Alisson, all my Liverpool-supporting friends have all been moaning about Alisson recently and his form, they do feel that he's starting to lose it.

"This would be a good replacement. However, I've got a funny feeling that the auction for a keeper like this may mean that Liverpool might not be the winners. I think Verbruggen might be looking at a club in Spain; that's my feeling."

Wyness previously told Football Insider last weekend that Verbruggen is likely to cost up to "£60million" for any club who wants to sign him next summer.

The Scottish businessman believes that Brighton will still charge "serious money" for their number one, despite his contract situation providing concern.

Wyness pointed towards the South Coast outfit's "excellent" management of their talents when it comes to sales, and he believes Verbruggen will be the next to join that list of lucrative sales.

Liverpool are clear admirers of the gloveman, but they will have to get in early next summer if they are to beat Real to a deal.

Pochettino questions ‘completely unfair’ situation Man United suffered against Liverpool
