Mauricio Pochettino believes Leny Yoro’s controversial early red card completely changed Manchester United’s 4-0 defeat to Liverpool in his first game in charge.

United were reduced to 10 men after only eight minutes when Yoro brought down Alexander Isak outside the penalty area.

Dominik Szoboszlai scored from the resulting free-kick before Florian Wirtz doubled Liverpool’s advantage minutes later, leaving Pochettino facing an almost impossible task at Anfield.

As detailed by Sky Sports, Pochettino strongly disagreed with the decision.

“The game changed completely after six minutes. The damage is done. It’s so painful. It was a completely unfair situation that happened…

“The ball was going to the keeper. If you review on the VAR, Yoro never had the intention to connect with Isak.

“When the control was wrong, and the ball was going to the keeper, then he [Isak] delayed a little bit his run and goes down. It wasn’t intentional [from Yoro]…

“For everyone who has played football, it’s clear that this was not a situation for a red card.”

Pochettino was equally frustrated by Jeremy Jacquet’s earlier challenge on Bryan Mbeumo.

“When you compare the actions [Jacquet and Yoro], that was a penalty, no? How it would have changed life, if you score after six minutes. For sure, we’d be talking in a different way.”

There is a legitimate debate over the red card. Isak had taken a heavy touch towards goalkeeper Senne Lammens, raising questions over whether he still had an obvious goalscoring opportunity.

However, the decision cannot completely excuse what followed.

A CaughtOffside report highlighted how quickly United collapsed, going two goals behind within 13 minutes.

Playing almost an entire match at Anfield with 10 men is extremely difficult, but Pochettino will know United must still defend better and remain more organised after setbacks.

The refereeing decisions undoubtedly shaped his debut. The bigger challenge now is ensuring one controversial moment does not become an explanation for United’s wider problems.

Mauricio Pochettino Slams 2 Referee Mistakes in Liverpool 4-0 Man Utd

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

New Manchester United manager Mauricio Pochettino is convinced the referee made two huge mistakes in his side's 4-0 defeat to Liverpool on Sunday afternoon.

All week, the former USMNT boss had been warned about just how difficult the task of taking over a Red Devils side currently languishing in the Premier League drop zone would be. Their latest defeat means United have now won just one of their opening 11 league matches of the season, while Pochettino has endured the worst start of any Man United manager in 100 years.

Pochettino's honeymoon period lasted just 10 minutes at Anfield, as Dominik Szoboszlai added insult to injury by smashing home the resulting free-kick following Leny Yoro's controversial red card for denying Alexander Isak a clear goalscoring opportunity.

That flashpoint has been the main talking point since the final whistle, with a VAR expert weighing in on the dismissal. However, Pochettino remains convinced his side were on the wrong end of another major decision from Sam Barrott, before the Reds ran riot with further goals from a resurgent Florian Wirtz, Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz.

The Argentine has called the decision to send Yoro off an 'unfair situation', which he brought up with the officials at full-time at Anfield. However, he was also quick to mention another incident that many supporters may have forgotten about.

Before Szoboszlai's opener, inside the first five minutes, Bryan Mbeumo was bundled over in the box after battling shoulder to shoulder with Jeremy Jacquet - and Pochettino believes there was enough contact for a penalty to be given. He said (watch his interview and the incident below):

"If you go two minutes before or one minute and a half before, the situation of Jacquet and Mbeumo, if you compare that, tell me how we are going to talk now about the team? Yes, of course, it's painful because it's 4-0 in the end and you lose the game, like we play more than 80 minutes with 10 men in an unfair...

"Of course, we need to be critical, but also the penalty to Mbeumo, that the action maybe was a mistake also of Liverpool, because we can make a mistake, no? But we were punished badly, and they were not punished. That is the truth."

It has since been confirmed that VAR did review the incident, but officials are believed to have deemed there was insufficient contact to warrant sending Barrott to the pitchside monitor or overturning the on-field decision themselves.

While sections of the United support, along with Pochettino, feel hard done by, another interpretation is that the Cameroon international may have simply kicked his own leg rather than been fouled, with Jacquet using his pace to get across him and block his route to goal.

There were some damning statistics that came out of Sunday's rout, and perhaps none summarised United's disastrous start to the season better than the fact they've conceded more goals than any other team in European football, having now shipped - or rather, 'invited' - 34 goals in all competitions.

The Argentine quickly learned the mess he's tasked with cleaning up.

On top of that, they now sit 19th in the Premier League table, just a point above rock bottom. There was no new manager bounce like there was with Michael Carrick, and how Pochettino will get a tune out of the current squad is anyone's guess - he has nine games to navigate with an average of three days rest each time before the January window opens.

But even then, will the top brass at Old Trafford give him the players he wants or deserves to work miracles? His predecessors would have something to say about that.

The numbers behind Pulisic’s influence on Milan’s scintillating attack

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Christian Pulisic has contributed to a goal every 87 minutes for Milan this season, while the Rossoneri have scored 28 goals in 12 league games, nearly a club record.

Milan secured a comfortable 5-1 win over Frosinone on Sunday, with Pulisic scoring in the second half.

The American has already scored five Serie A goals this season and, according to Gazzetta, has contributed to a goal every 87 minutes through either a goal or an assist.

He has also scored in each of his last three Serie A appearances. The only time he had a longer scoring streak for Milan was between September and October 2024, when he found the net in four consecutive matches.

Pulisic’s scintillating performances have contributed to Milan’s strong attacking displays, with eight different players scoring in Serie A so far.

The Rossoneri have scored 28 goals in 12 games; only twice in history have they achieved more: 29 under Nereo Rocco and Lajos Czeizler, who coached Milan in the 1950s.

Lorenzo Bettoni is the Editor of Football Italia.

Man United planning 'big capital event' after new appointment: 'Watch this one closely...'

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Manchester United's ownership are planning to make another significant investment in the club's infrastructure within the next 12 months.

That is according to former Everton chief executive Keith Wyness, speaking exclusively to Football Insider, who believes that the signs point towards another big project at Old Trafford.

This comes amid the news that the Red Devils are advertising for a new financial director, as they look set to add another figure to their backroom team.

Wyness believes this is a signal that a "big capital event" could be approaching on the horizon for United.

The club hierarchy are facing heavy criticism once again this season, with the team currently sitting inside the relegation zone.

The optimism surrounding Mauricio Pochettino's arrival as manager was quickly quashed by a heavy 4-0 defeat away at Liverpool on Sunday.

Everton’s former chief Wyness – who served as CEO at Goodison Park between 2004 and 2009 and now runs a football consultancy advising elite clubs – believes that United fans should be watching the new financial director appointment "closely."

The Scottish businessman expects the role to entail close management of the club's coffers, ensuring that money is being used efficiently and in the right areas.

Speaking on the latest edition of Football Insider's Inside Track podcast, Wyness explained that it could be a sign of a big change coming at United.

"I don't know if it's so much another layer, but I do tend to read into financial hirings as being quite important," he said.

"Now we've talked about Chelsea hiring a special accountant the other day for special SCR issues and keeping track of those sorts of things. It could be as simple as that, but I think there could be more to it.

"I think this could be sort of signalling that there's going to be a big capital event of some description going forward with United coming up.

"We'll be watching this one closely as to what level they hire the CFO at and what skill sets he has in his background. That will give us an indication of where things might be going. It's about being a good financial detective. I think we should be watching this one quite closely."

Football Insider revealed on Monday that Pochettino holds the key to United sealing a move to sign Villarreal right-back Alex Freeman.

Defensive reinforcements are a top priority for the club this winter, and the USA international has been identified as an ideal candidate.

Freeman has already started seven games in La Liga so far this season, and has also gained Champions League experience with Villarreal.

Sources say that Pochettino is a "big fan" of the 22-year-old, having relied on him heavily during his time in charge of the USA.

Barcelona are considering Manchester United defender Lisandro Martínez as they look to strengthen the centre of their defence during the January transfer window.

The Argentina international has been linked with a move away from Old Trafford amid uncertainty over his long-term future, and his contract situation could make him one of the more affordable experienced options available to Barcelona.

However, the Catalan club appear to have some reservations over whether Martínez completely fits the physical profile they want.

According to Cadena SER, Barcelona are actively looking at centre-back options following renewed concerns over their defensive performances.

Martínez is among the names being considered alongside Inter Milan defender Alessandro Bastoni and Real Betis centre-back Natan.

His situation at Man United could make him financially attractive because he is approaching the end of his contract, although United still have leverage over his future.

The report also claims some figures at Barcelona have doubts about Martínez because of his height, with the 1.75m defender not necessarily fitting the physical profile they want for a new centre-back.

A recent CaughtOffside report also named Martínez on Barcelona’s shortlist, noting that Hansi Flick wants a left-footed defender who can bring greater balance to his back line.

From Man United’s perspective, this is a situation that needs careful handling.

Martínez has endured difficult periods with injuries, but when fit he still offers aggression, progressive passing and the ability to build attacks from the back. Those qualities could also appeal to Mauricio Pochettino, particularly if United intend to play more aggressively and dominate possession.

The biggest issue is value. If Barcelona view Martínez primarily as a low-cost opportunity because of his contract, United should resist accepting a cut-price January deal simply to avoid uncertainty.

The club are already expected to strengthen at centre-back, so selling Martínez could make sense if a replacement arrives first.

But allowing an experienced international defender to leave cheaply would only create another problem.

Barcelona’s interest is worth monitoring, yet Man United should only consider a January exit if the financial offer and replacement plan both make sense.

L'attaquant sénégalais de 31 ans, l'ancien Vert Ibrahima Wadji, avait  claqué son premier pion de la saison 5 minutes après son entrée en jeu lors du match nul de son équipe Turan Tovuk sur le terrain d'Araz Nakhchivan en septembre dernier. Welcome to Man United, Mauricio Pochettino. It’s awful

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Liverpool 4-0 Manchester United: It took a 13-minute crash course for Pochettino to realise what he was in for as United were hammered by their rivals on his bow

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Welcome to Manchester United, Mauricio. For a man who had coveted the United job for years, this was an ignominious introduction. Mauricio Pochettino got a 13-minute crash course in the craziness of his new club. His first game was lost before he made his first substitution, and that came as early as the 22nd-minute. His debut brought the joint heaviest ever defeat by a United manager in his first game in charge, and the biggest in 100 years.

“So painful,” Pochettino said. “So disappointed.” As can be the way with United of late, most things that could go wrong did. When their new head coach’s name was sung, it was by the Kop. “Poch is at the wheel,” they taunted. A first meaningful day of work showed the scale of the task to steer United in the right direction.

The team who exhibited the pace and energy of the best Pochettino sides were Liverpool, relishing the chance to compound their rivals’ problems. Florian Wirtz scored a glorious goal, Bradley Barcola kept demonstrating a sprinter’s speed and Rio Ngumoha illuminated Anfield with his precocity. Yet each was aided by United, hapless and hopeless, showing it is easier to change the manager than to reverse one of the most extraordinary declines in English football history.

It was as though Pochettino’s charges had decided to show him a snapshot of the problems: their self-destructive streak, their defensive failings, their capacity to concede in quick succession. No previous Premier League manager had seen his side both let in two goals and go down to 10 men in the first half of his opening game. Pochettino’s United accomplished that in a quarter of an hour. Suffice to say there was no honeymoon period.

The watching Sir Alex Ferguson had lost his first game in charge, 2-0 at Oxford 40 years ago. Pochettino topped that, in the wrong way. Only Lal Hilditch, in 1926, had begun his reign as badly, with another 4-0 loss.

And yet there was a continuity to United’s calamity. Meet the new boss, same as the old boss? Despite regime change, some of this was very familiar. United were two goals behind after 18 minutes against Roma, after four against Villa, after 13 against Liverpool. United got rid of Michael Carrick, kept the masterplan of going 2-0 down and discovered it has certain flaws.

This was scarcely an unlucky 13 for United. Rather, they were culpable in their own downfall. Matthijs de Ligt needlessly left Ronald Araujo’s long ball, Alexander Isak ran behind the United defence and Leny Yoro barged him over. “It was an unfair situation,” complained Pochettino. “It was clear that was not a red card.” Iraola disagreed. “It is clearly the last man, clearly a foul,” he said.

So Yoro was sent off by referee Sam Barrott and waved off by the Liverpool faithful. The punishment for United was doubled when Dominik Szoboszlai drilled his free kick past a ramshackle wall and in. Chants of “going down” rang around Anfield. It was replaced, a few minutes later, by “sacked in the morning”.

With wonderful improvisation, Wirtz hooked in a volley after Ngumoha sped past Diogo Dalot and dinked in a cross. If a criticism was that the £100m German had influenced too few major matches in his Liverpool career, he altered this, brilliantly, and ended with an assist to accompany his second goal in as many games. “He's in a lovely moment,” said Iraola.

Liverpool slowed down at the end of the first half and then sped up again after the break. Barcola’s profligate finishing had cost him Liverpool’s third goal but his extraordinary pace created it. The £106m man’s scorched-earth solo run was followed by a pass to allow Ngumoha to replace Michael Owen as Liverpool’s youngest ever scorer against United. The teenager had tormented Dalot, but he was not the only winger on the scoresheet.

Victor Munoz exchanged passes with Wirtz and curled in the fourth after Liverpool’s high pressing was shown as Virgil van Dijk robbed Joshua Zirkzee, 25 yards from the United goal. Iraola ended doing fist pumps to the Kop, Jurgen Klopp-style.

“We probably should have scored more than four,” Iraola said. Senne Lammens saved, from Isak, Alexis Mac Allister and Szoboszlai. Harry Maguire made a brilliant block to turn an Isak shot away. The Swede slashed a shot wide after De Ligt inexplicably missed the ball for a second time.

It reflected badly on the new manager. Perhaps Pochettino’s first big call was to bring in De Ligt for a first league appearance of 2026. That backfired. Meanwhile, Maguire was first dropped and then summoned.

Pochettino had to keep changing tack. As Liverpool scored four goals, United used four formations. Pochettino seemed to begin by borrowing Ruben Amorim’s 3-4-3, then it looked a 4-2-3-1. Then it was neither, with United down to 10 men. “After what happened, happened and the plan was in the bin,” said Pochettino, who went to first 4-4-1 then 5-3-1, and who tried to argue what he felt was an injustice of a red card rendered what followed rather irrelevant. “We concede four goals with 10 men, and in a circumstance after playing 80 minutes at Anfield,” he said. But the circumstances are that United are in the relegation zone, lost on Pochettino’s bow, and were walloped by their rivals.

Blackhawks’ Wyatt Kaiser suspended for dirty hit to head of Flames player

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Blackhawks' Wyatt Kaiser suspended for dirty hit to head of Flames player appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Things have not gone well for the Chicago Blackhawks this year. They are 7-14-2 this year, and the Blackhawks recently lost Connor Bedard to injury. The Blackhawks took a 4-3 win in overtime against the Calgary Flames on Saturday.

In that game, Wyatt Kaiser, a defenseman for the Blackhawks, hit Martin Pospisil late and in the head. The Flames forward needed help off the ice, and Kaiser was given a five-minute major for a hit to the head, which was held up by video review. He was also given a game misconduct and removed from the game. There was a scrum that followed, which resulted in a two-minute minor for roughing for Kaiser, and also for Matvei Gridin and Ryan Strome of the Flames. Now, the NHL has decided on punishment for Kaiser, per Frank Seravalli.

"Kaiser suspended 3 games by @NHLPlayerSafety," Seravalli posted on X, formerly Twitter.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

This is the first suspension for Kaiser, who broke into the NHL in 2022-23. He became a full-time NHL player last year, playing in 77 games with six goals and 11 assists. The 24-year-old has played 23 games this year with two goals and five assists.

He will miss the next three games for the Blackhawks. That starts on Sunday night against the Vancouver Canucks. They will then play the Seattle Kraken on the road Wednesday night, and the New York Rangers on Friday at home. Kaiser will be eligible to return against the Minnesota Wild next Sunday afternoon. With the suspension, Kaiser will lose just over 26 thousand in salary.

Meanwhile, Pospisil, who has a history of concussions, will likely miss some time.

PIAA FOOTBALL: North Pocono, Scranton Prep, Dunmore on road for quarterfinals

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

North Pocono, Scranton Prep and Dunmore hit the road as the PIAA football playoffs enter the quarterfinals Friday.

North Pocono (12-1), the District 2 Class 4A champion, takes on District 6 champion Bellefonte (6-5) at Danville High School at 7 p.m.

In Class 3A, District 2 champion Scranton Prep (12-1) meets District 3 champion Wyomissing (9-2) at North Schuylkill High School at 7 p.m.

Undefeated Dunmore (13-0), the District 2 Class 2A champion, plays District 12 champion West Catholic (10-1) at Pleasant Valley High School at 7 p.m.

North Pocono reached the quarterfinals, winning its second straight District 2 title last Friday in a 26-7 win over Dallas. Bellefonte defeated District 4 champion Shikellamy, 38-21, in the PIAA first round.

Scranton Prep opened the state playoffs with a 49-7 win over Archbishop Wood. Wyomissing Area defeated Annville-Cleona, 31-10, in the District 3 championship game last week.

The Cavaliers improved their state-playoff record to 6-9. They lost to Wyomissing Area in 2021 (35-14).

Dunmore won its 13th straight game by the 35-point running clock mercy rule with a 42-7 win over Northern Lehigh in the PIAA first round. West Catholic defeated District 3 champion Upper Dauphin, 46-7.

The Bucks improved to 17-14 in PIAA playoff games all-time. They have played West Catholic four times. West Catholic won in 2008 (49-21), 2009 (13-0), 2015 (28-10), and 2018 (21-3).

Why was Leny Yoro sent off against Liverpool?

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Yoro was sent off inside eight minutes before Liverpool scored from the subsequent free-kick

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Life at Manchester United got off to a nightmare start for Mauricio Pochettino as his side was reduced to 10 men inside eight minutes in his managerial bow against Liverpool.

Liverpool started brightly and fashioned a chance out of nothing when Ronald Araujo’s hooked clearance left Matthijs de Ligt - making his first league start in a year - in no man’s land.

Alexander Isak latched onto the ball but was clattered into last man Leny Yoro just outside the box, prompting referee Sam Barrett to reach into his pocket and show the Frenchman a red card.

"It doesn't matter if you're playing with a back four, or back five, you've got to do the basics right. De Ligt - I'm amazed with him at this level,” Roy Keane said on Sky Sports.

"I know he's come back from an injury, he's just not reading the flight of the ball. If you do get it wrong, you run back, you sprint back, and then Yoro has got to be covering. If you're a defender, you always expect the worst."

There was a debate over whether VAR should have overturned the decision due to Isak’s heavy touch, which put into question he would have kept control of the ball - a key consideration in determining if a player has denied an obvious goalscoring opportunity (DOGSO).

"Isak's not getting the ball. There's no way he's getting the ball,” Keane added.

"It's clumsy from United. Obviously it's one straight ball over the top. You shouldn't be in so much trouble.

"But there's no way he's getting that ball. Absolutely not. I know Isak is quick and he's sharp, but we've looked at it again.

"It's really poor defending. Players not smelling danger. But Isak is not getting that ball. Absolutely not."

But VAR John Brooks did not call for a review, with the Premier League Match Centre writing in a statement: “The referee’s call of red card to Yoro for denial of an obvious goal-scoring opportunity (DOGSO) was checked and confirmed by VAR.

“REF: ‘It's a red card for DOGSO. On-field decision is outside [the box], red card, DOGSO’.

“VAR: ‘Defender bundles into him into the back, gets too tight, I think it's a foul’.”

Things then went from bad to worse for United as Dominik Szoboszlai stepped up to the free-kick and fired past Senne Lammens into the bottom-left corner, ensuring Yoro’s blunder was fully punished.

Liverpool doubled their lead just three minutes later, with Florian Wirtz volleying high into the goal as Pochettino’s side rapidly unravelled.

Liverpool’s outright domination of the opening stages, combined with losing a defender, sparked Pochettino into making an early change, replacing forward Bryan Mbeumo with centre-back Harry Maguire in the 22nd minute.

‘Sacked in the morning’: Mauricio Pochettino taunted by Liverpool fans during catastrophic Man Utd bow

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Pochettino started life as Man United manager with a 4-0 hammering at the hands of Liverpool

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

The job of Manchester United head coach has become something of a poisoned chalice for a succession of managers but even new boss Mauricio Pochettino could not have expected how quickly he would be taunted with jokes about him being sacked.

His reign began in humiliating fashion after an early red card and two goals led to the Kop chanting “Sacked in the morning” just 13 minutes into the Argentinian’s first game.

Chants of “Going down with the City” when the third goal went in – followed by “You’re not famous any more” and “Poch is at the wheel” – will only have made the trip back along the M62 more uncomfortable.

The match finished 4-0 when Victor Munoz slotted home in the 82nd minute, putting the seal on a disastrous bow in the United dugout for Pochettino.

"So painful, so disappointed,” Pochettino told Sky Sports. “It's not ok to start a game in the Premier League with this situation. The game changed completely after six minutes. Now we can explain everything but the damage is done, it's so painful.

"The players are so, so feeling the pain of the result. I told them to the calm: it was an unfair situation that happened. We need to accept that reality. We need to try to increase our focus, concentration and make better decisions on the field.

"We also need to be critical on ourselves but some situations happened in the game that we are not happy."

United’s all-time great boss Sir Alex Ferguson had a seat in the directors’ box alongside the actor and fan James Nesbitt – in Liverpool recording a new Poirot television show – and it was more a case of boiling blood than ‘Cold Feet’ at Anfield.

Ferguson had shared a pre-match joke with fellow Scot and long-time Liverpool rival Sir Kenny Dalglish about their choice of chocolate, but that was about as funny as it got for the 84-year-old.

There were also some stony faces from chief executive officer Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox, sat immediately in front of Ferguson, after a shambolic start for the new head coach.

Roy Keane accuses Man United fans of ‘applauding rubbish’ in Liverpool hammering

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Mauricio Pochettino’s first game in charge got off to the worst possible start when Leny Yoro was red-carded after eight minutes

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Roy Keane accused visiting Manchester United supporters at Anfield of “applauding rubbish” after Liverpool romped to a 4-0 win.

Mauricio Pochettino’s first game in charge got off to the worst possible start when Leny Yoro was red-carded after eight minutes.

Dominik Szoboszlai and Florian Wirtz made it 2-0 to Liverpool inside 13 minutes, after which United’s 10 men never looked like responding.

Two goals in the second half from Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz further laid bare the size of the task facing Pochettino as United were left second-bottom of the Premier League.

Some travelling fans offered encouraging applause to the players at the end, a reflection of perhaps of the losing cause they had fought after losing Yoro so early but, speaking on Sky Sports, Keane suggested it was a mark of how low standards have fallen.

“Has a team ever got a result before with 10 men? It can be done,” he said, taking issue with a comment made by Gary Neville highlighting the importance of the red.

“Are you telling me Man Utd couldn’t have done better and everyone is saying it’s not bad. At least show a bit more fight, a bit more quality, I can’t believe what I’m hearing.

“Fans are applauding the players, applauding what? Applauding rubbish. When do the players take responsibility for some of the decision-making? Liverpool should have scored eight or nine.

“I feel like swearing. How can anyone say 4-0 is not too bad?”

Keane fumed over United’s failure to get the basics right on a dire afternoon, with Matthijs de Ligt in particular struggling in his first league start in over a year.

"If you do the basics right, you give yourself a bit of a chance so no matter how good you think you might be or a big club you are, if you're not doing the basics right, you're going to be constantly under pressure and constantly getting criticised,” he added.

"If that happened against a pub team, they'd be criticising you and this is a big game.

"You're talking about starting well, being solid, but again, we go back to the defensive shape and one stray ball over the top puts somebody in on goal.

Leny Yoro wrongly sent off, Fernandes rages: Three Sam Barrott howlers in Liverpool vs Man United

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Senior Correspondent AUTHORITY Senior football correspondent and podcast host with specialist knowledge across the Premier League and SPFL. FOCUS Transfer news, breaking stories, and behind-the-scenes insight across Arsenal, Liverpool, Celtic, and Manchester United. THE INSIGHT Callum utilises a network of club and industry contacts across the Premier League and SPFL to deliver verified, high-speed reporting. He provides the depth behind the headlines — from breaking transfer news to behind-the-scenes developments — ensuring fans get the full story as it happens.

Sam Barrott was a busy man in the middle of the park as he oversaw Liverpool's win against Manchester United.

The 34-year-old took charge of the fiery derby clash at Anfield in what proved to be a difficult day for the officials live on Sky Sports.

Barrott was immediately given a big call to make as Leny Yoro was sent off after just eight minutes after crashing into Alexander Isak when he was through on goal.

The resulting free kick was slammed home by Dominik Szoboszlai, and just three minutes later, Florian Wirtz volleyed the ball in to give Andoni Iraola's side a 2-0 lead.

Goals from Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz made it 4-0 in the second half as Liverpool eased away from their rivals, sparking further concerns about the Red Devils' form.

Barrott, however, could find himself at the centre of many of the discussions and while the referee was far from the one to blame, some fans are not happy.

As previously mentioned, the biggest flashpoint in the game came after just eight minutes, as Yoro was shown a straight red card for bringing down Isak on the edge of the box.

While it seemed a relatively straightforward decision at the time, replays quickly showed it was more subjective, as it may not have been a clear opportunity.

The striker's touch was well out of his feet, and questions have been asked about whether he would have met it ahead of the on-rushing Senne Lammens.

To illustrate how difficult the decision was, former PGMOL chief Keith Hackett first told Football Insider that the decision was clear, before reviewing footage which later made him dub the call "subjective".

Yoro's red card was not the only decision which left the Man United fans, players and coaches frustrated, as Bruno Fernandes confronted the referee later in the game.

In the 57th minute, the Portugal international raced after a ball which split the Liverpool defence, only to collide with the covering Jeremy Jacquet.

A free kick was given in favour of the Liverpool defender, launching Fernandes into an explosion of frustration towards the referee after he felt he was hard-done-by.

The attacking midfielder insisted the contact was shoulder-to-shoulder, while also suggesting he had his shirt pulled, but his explosive cries fell on deaf ears.

In the end, Fernandes was probably fortunate to avoid a yellow card for his outburst.

Another decision which went against Man United at Anfield came earlier in the first half, although with Pochettino's side 2-0 down at the time, it would not have changed much.

In the 25th minute, a ball was fizzed into the feet of Matheus Cunha in midfield, as as he attempted to take it on the turn, Alexis Mac Allister slid through the back of him.

Despite winning the ball, the Argentina midfielder's trailing leg caught the back foot of Cunha, leaving him in a heap, but Barrott was not moved to brandish his card.

It was another of multiple decisions which went against the Red Devils, and while they cannot be used as excuses, they will feel harshly treated by the match officials.

Prediction: Galatasaray vs Aston Villa

Turning their attention back to Champions League action on Tuesday evening, Aston Villa will travel to Istanbul when they meet Turkish giants Galatasaray.

With a stacked schedule from across the continent in midweek, there are plenty of eye-catching Champions League betting promotions available.

Ahead of a blockbuster battle from Istanbul on Tuesday, you can find all of the key Galatasaray vs Aston Villa H2H stats here.

Starting with the hosts, marking their return from November’s International break with a 2-1 victory at home against Samsunspor over the weekend, Galatasaray are enjoying what has become a dominant run of form. Already holding a commanding six-point buffer at the top of the Super Lig standings and universally regarded as a landslide contender to once again defend their Turkish crown, Okan Buruk’s men certainly find the mood in their camp at a red-hot high. In fact, last seen in Champions League action opening November with a deserved 2-0 win at home against Stuttgart, Galatasaray have won each of their previous seven consecutive appearances across all competitions. Likewise, gaining a reputation for their free-scoring performances in the final third, Buruk’s high-flying squad have notched 19 goals on their seven-match winning run.

Undergoing minor knee surgery earlier in the month, Galatasaray will once again be without talismanic striker Victor Osimhen on Tuesday evening. Likewise, on what has a huge blow for Buruk’s camp, the Turkish giants will also be without blockbuster summer recruit Rafael Leao due to a recent muscle issue.

Left as an unusused substitute against Samsunspor over the weekend, one-time Tottenham defender Davinson Sanchez could be handed a full recall. Now in his fifth campaign at Rams Park, former Arsenal midfielder Lucas Torreira will start in the middle of the park.

As for the visitors, marking their respective return on Saturday afternoon with a downbeat display in the Midlands as they were eventually forced to settle for a 0-0 stalemate against Sunderland, Aston Villa find themselves at the heart of what has become an extended wobble. Heading into November’s fortnight interval with an infamous last-gasp collapse at Old Trafford as they played out a thrilling 4-4 draw against Manchester United, Unai Emery’s men have failed to win any of their previous five appearances across all competitions (two defeats, three draws). Blown away in Catalonia earlier in the month as they opened November with a routine 3-0 drumming against Spanish heavyweights Barcelona, Aston Villa now sit in the bottom-half of the Premier League standings. It should also be highlighted, Emery’s struggling squad have shipped 10 goals in their last three showdowns away from Villa Park.

Yet to make a maiden appearance this season after sustaining a long-term knee injury while away with Belgium at this summer’s World Cup, Aston Villa will once again be without ex-Everton star Amadou Onana. Likewise, Italian defender Matteo Ruggeri is still recovering from a muscle issue and won’t play any part in Istanbul.

Forced to settle for a spot on the bench over the weekend, club captain John McGinn will be handed an immediate full recall. Enjoying a sensational start to life in the Midlands and also notching three goals in his opening four Champions League appearances, summer recruit Nicolas Jackson will spearhead Villa’s attack.

Really showing some cause for concern on either side of November’s international break, Aston Villa should be bracing themselves for another stern examination here. Completely blown away in Catalonia earlier in the month as they fell to that 3-0 loss against Barcelona, Tuesday’s guests have failed to win any of their previous five consecutive appearances across all competitions. Meeting a free-scoring Galatasaray who are enjoying a stunning extended flurry, Emery’s men have shipped 10 goals in their last three matchups outside of the Midlands and they could be asked some serious questions on foreign soil.

Galatasaray vs Aston Villa - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Joshua Cole | 22 Nov 2026 19:53

Galatasaray will be looking to continue their resurgence in the UEFA Champions League when they welcome Aston Villa to Rams Park on Tuesday evening, with the Turkish champions aiming to close the gap on the top eight.

The hosts sit 18th in the league-phase standings with six points from four matches, while Villa occupy sixth place on nine points and will be looking to return to winning ways after a difficult run of results.

Galatasaray head into this fixture in encouraging form after winning seven consecutive matches across all competitions, with their recent improvement helping them maintain their position at the top of the Turkish Super Lig while also strengthening their Champions League qualification prospects.

Okan Buruk's side have won their last two UCL matches, recording a 4-2 victory away to Lille before following that result with a 2-0 home triumph over VfB Stuttgart.x

The victories helped steady the ship after Galatasaray had suffered defeats against Sporting CP and Barcelona in their opening two Champions League fixtures, leaving them with little margin for error in their pursuit of a place in the knockout stages.

They have also continued their impressive run domestically, winning their last six league matches, a run that includes a win over Fenerbahce, and most recently a 2-1 win over Samsunspor 2-1 at the weekend.

Galatasaray have also been particularly impressive at home this season, winning seven of their nine matches at Rams Park while drawing one and losing one, with Barcelona the only side to defeat them on their own turf.

They will now have the opportunity to equal their best-ever run of consecutive victories in the European Cup or Champions League by recording a third straight success.

However, their defensive record remains a concern, having conceded nine goals in their opening four Champions League matches, including four in their home defeat against Barcelona and two against Lille.

Meanwhile, Villa arrive in Istanbul having experienced a significant dip in form, with their five-game winless run following a period during which they were without defeat in eight straight games, winning seven of those matches.

Unai Emery's side began that difficult sequence with a 3-0 loss away to Bournemouth in the League Cup, before subsequently drawing 1-1 with Fulham, which preceded a disappointing result in the Champions League as they lost 3-0 away to Barcelona.

Villa then produced an entertaining but defensively concerning performance in a 4-4 draw against Manchester United, before a goalless draw with Sunderland in their last outing.

Nevertheless, Villa have made an encouraging start to their Champions League campaign, winning three of their opening four matches to collect nine points and remain among the teams occupying the automatic qualification positions.

Their European campaign has included a particularly impressive 6-0 victory against Fenerbahce, which represented their widest margin of victory in European competition against a Turkish opponent, and they will be eager to rediscover that level of performance in Istanbul.

Both teams have never met in competitive football, making Tuesday's encounter the first meeting between the sides, although the hosts have traditionally proved difficult opponents for English clubs playing in Istanbul.

English teams have failed to win in nine of their last 10 visits to Galatasaray, losing six and drawing three, while Villa have lost just once in eight European matches against Turkish opposition, winning six and drawing one.

Galatasaray are expected to be without Victor Osimhen, who underwent knee surgery after sustaining an injury during their Champions League victory over Stuttgart.

Rafael Leao is also sidelined with a muscle injury, while Roland Sallai and Ismail Jakobs are carrying fitness concerns and could miss Tuesday's encounter.

Aleksey Batrakov, who has scored four goals in the Champions League this season, three of which have been at RAMS Park, will be the man to watch in Osimhen’s absence.

Villa will be without Matty Cash, who sustained a concussion while on international duty with Poland, while Amadou Onana and Matteo Ruggeri are also expected to remain sidelined through injury.

Victor Lindelof is a doubt after missing the weekend draw against Sunderland due to illness, while Aaron Wan-Bissaka has returned from a hamstring problem and could be available for selection.

Nicolas Jackson is expected to lead the attack, with John McGinn, Emiliano Buendia and Johan Manzambi likely to provide support as Emery looks to end Villa's winless run.

Cakir; Singo, Sanchez, Bardakci, Elmali; Torreira, Lemina; Akgun, Batrakov, Yilmaz; Gul

Suzuki; Bogarde, Harwood-Bellis, Pau Torres, Maatsen; Kamara, Joao Gomes; McGinn, Buendia, Manzambi; Jackson

Galatasaray have been in impressive form in recent weeks and their home performances should give them confidence of securing another positive result against Villa.

However, the hosts will be without several important attacking players potentially limiting their options in the final third, and while Villa have struggled defensively during their recent winless run, they have enough in attack to play out an entertaining draw in Istanbul.

Andoni Iraola fires jab at Mauricio Pochettino after easy Liverpool win over Manchester United

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Liverpool manager Andoni Iraola said his side could “celebrate at half time” during their 4-0 win over Manchester United.

The Reds ran out easy victors against their fierce rivals on Sunday, with United hoping to snap a miserable run of just one win in 12 matches.

They did not get the desired bounce from the arrival of new boss Mauricio Pochettino, who watched his side thrashed at Anfield and go down to ten men in eight minutes after Leny Yoro’s red card, which he bemoaned afterwards.

Dominik Szoboszlai, Florian Wirtz, Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz netted the goals to hand the hosts a straightforward three points that sees them sit fourth, seven points off league leaders Arsenal.

United, meanwhile, are spiralling and remain in the Premier League relegation zone, in 19th place.

Iraola seemed to fire a jab at Pochettino and United in his post-match interview.

"It's a big win, always against United it is a big game,” he said.

“I think you can enjoy it, you can see it coming. It's difficult to enjoy in the Premier League, normally you suffer even if you win, but I think we could celebrate at half time and enjoy it a little bit and have lovely moments.

"We could have scored more. We missed some clear chances today. In other games you will remember those chances, today we've controlled the game and everything was happening around the box.

"It's a 4-0 [win] that was good for [our confidence], trying to get better results here at home."

Zlatan Ibrahimovic Gave a Strong Verdict on Playing in the MLS

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

MLS fans will be intrigued to hear what Zlatan Ibrahimovic once had to say about the level of competition the league provides after Thomas Muller's recent assessment.

Muller arrived in the MLS in the summer of 2025, joining the Vancouver Whitecaps after cementing his legacy as an all-time great in European football with Bayern Munich. At the age of 37, he quickly became one of the best players in the United States alongside the likes of Inter Miami's Lionel Messi and Chicago Fire's Robert Lewandowski.

There has been a growing interest in Major League Soccer, not least because of Messi's arrival in 2023, but also the money on offer to European stars who are in the twilight years of their career. And Muller seemed to suggest during a rant that the MLS doesn't come anywhere close to rivalling the world's best leagues because of a lack of risk and pressure.

Muller gave an honest assessment of the MLS and believes the reason that it can't be compared to other leagues is because of its system. He said: "There is no relegation. There is no big pressure. There is... 'oh yeah we do a rebuild'. Nobody's happy to lose, but it's totally different when you're fighting relegation."

The former Germany international acknowledged that MLS clubs "lose budget for the next year" and that has a knock-on effect for those working at the club. He added that the absence of promotion and relegation also leads to "careless" defending and a far wider gap in consistency compared to playing in Europe.

This candid interview reignited debates about the MLS' closed-league structure and its perception in Europe. There were some within the MLS and Whitecaps fanbase who supported Muller's verdict, believing he's correct to suggest that the system leads to a lack of high-stakes drama unlike in European football relegation battles.

Muller isn't afraid to speak his mind, much like Ibrahimovic who was one of the first major household names to swap Europe for the MLS. The legendary former Swedish striker spent around 20 months at LA Galaxy at the end of his career before returning to Europe and retiring at AC Milan.

Ibrahimovic's opinions on the MLS were of a similar nature, questioning the mentality of its fans. The former Manchester United frontman said in June of this year:

“Most of my career I played in Italy, France, Spain, England… then I came to the U.S. I think the U.S. had the softest fans."

Ibrahimovic also wasn't fond of the reaction from fans to their club losing a game: "When we were losing games, people would say, ‘Don’t worry, we just need to make the playoffs.’ While also adding: “I’m like… what do you mean, don’t worry? In my world, I am worried."

During the Swede's time at LA Galaxy he spoke openly about the differences between the MLS and the Premier League. He told ESPN that the difference in "pace" is most noticeable, but also "the risk" and "the quality," vowing to try and help it improve on these fronts.

Ibrahimovic highlighted factors such as the climate in the United States compared with in Europe and the UK. Like Muller, he took to the MLS with ease and throughout his time in the USA, found the back of the net 53 times in 58 games.

Unlike most major leagues around the world, the MLS has an incredibly different format which can prove confusing.

Despite Muller's comments, there are no immediate plans for the MLS to change its rules so that promotion and relegation come into effect. There are several obstacles that prevent its leaders from doing so, particularly because it is a closed single-entity league with owners paying massive expansion fees.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Gary Neville delivers scathing Manchester United verdict after heavy Liverpool defeat

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Gary Neville believes there has been a “remarkable” drop in Manchester United’s standards after the Red Devils sank to a 4-0 thrashing by Liverpool on Sunday.

The club were looking to propel themselves out of the Premier League relegation zone, helped by a bounce from the arrival of new manager Mauricio Pochettino, who took charge of his first match at Anfield.

The Argentine, however, was dealt a nightmare start, as Leny Yoro was sent off inside eight minutes for denying Alexander Isak a clear goalscoring opportunity, and Dominik Szoboszlai, Florian Wirtz, Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz got the goals for Andoni Iraola’s brilliant hosts.

Pochettino bemoaned Yoro’s red card as “unfair” in his immediate post-match reaction, but he could not complain about his side’s performance on the night, as they were swept aside emphatically by their historic rivals.

On Sky Sports, former United defender Neville said that Pochettino had now seen “the worst” of his new club’s predicament.

“It's really desperate and it's tiring watching them,” he said.

“This is the worst Manchester United have ever been in 53 years.

“There's no time to work on this. Maybe this will be a good thing in the long run that Pochettino sees the worst of it.

"He's got a big job on here. There'll be an element of 'Let's just forget about this game because Yoro got sent off and it's with 10 men', but it rides into the game in midweek.

"From a defensive perspective, I've never seen anything like this.

"I grew up watching great defenders that were fantastic players that were aggressive. They were brilliant people, great fighters and had spirit.

"You watch this lot, honestly, it's unbelievable. They're going to have to advance money to [Pochettino] in January for defenders. They're probably going to need another couple next summer.

"Forget the rest of the squad for now, they've got to build from the back.

"Manchester United are the worst team in Europe defensively right now. You can't say anything more than that. It's unbelievable. 34 goals [conceded] in 15 matches.

"This is where we've got to where we're starting to write off 4-0 defeats at Anfield as if it's just the norm and what should be accepted.

"The club standards have dropped so far, it's incredible.

"But the fans’ standards haven't dropped. They expect a Manchester United team to be competitive every single week, and what they're seeing now, Manchester United have got the worst defence in the whole of Europe, and are currently second from bottom in the Premier League.

The 15 best, funniest reactions to Liverpool handing Pochettino & Man Utd yet another hiding

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Manchester United‘s new era under Mauricio Pochettino got off to about as bad a start as imaginable, with Liverpool handing them a humiliating 4-0 defeat at Anfield.

There was a sense of deja vu about it all, given how often United have been on the receiving end of a Liverpool thumping in recent years.

It was a nightmare start for the Pochettino reign. The visitors were down to 10 men inside eight minutes after Leny Yoro was sent off for bringing down Alexander Isak. Dominik Szoboszlai promptly converted the resulting free-kick before Florian Wirtz doubled Liverpool’s advantage with a superb volley.

Wirtz was the standout performer, producing a goal and an assist in a masterclass performance that saw him create a club-record 10 chances. It was surely his best display yet in a Liverpool shirt.

Rio Ngumoha added a third after the break as Liverpool made their numerical advantage count, while late substitute Victor Munoz completed another thoroughly miserable afternoon for the Red Devils at Anfield.

Matthijs de Ligt, meanwhile, had a day to forget on his first Premier League start in 12 months, enduring a defensive disasterclass as Pochettino’s big call backfired.

“We need to adapt,” Pochettino responded in his post-match interview with BBC Sport.

“It’s true that a difficult decision that we maybe don’t agree with, but we cannot talk too much about the game. 11 v 10 from the beginning of the game. In the way we approached the game and we wanted to play, it’s normal to affect all the preparation.

“It’s so painful, we feel pain for that defeat, but we need to keep the head up and translate that anger to an energy to start to work from tomorrow for the next game.”

Augsburg homegrown talent Tim Schnitzer seizes opportunity in vital Bundesliga win

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Eighteen-year-old FC Augsburg academy prospect Tim Schnitzer enjoyed an unforgettable afternoon on Sunday, making his first Bundesliga start in his hometown club’s 3-1 victory over Mainz 05. A defensive injury crisis forced head coach Manuel Baum to deploy the youngster in an unfamiliar role, but Schnitzer delivered an impressively composed performance that earned special praise from his trainer.

Speaking to Get German Football News and other journalists in the mixed zone after the match, Schnitzer called it a “dream come true to start where I used to be a ball boy”. The Augsburg native admitted to some nervousness ahead of his starting XI debut. Schnitzer revealed that he had slept less than usual the night before and even awoken before his alarm clock.

“I was definitely a little nervous,” Schnitzer conceded.

The Germany youth international nevertheless settled into his role on the left side of Augsburg’s back-three relatively quickly, displaying maturity and confidence in possession. Schnitzer won 70 percent of his ground challenges and connected with 89 percent of his passes, according to Opta data.

GGFN considered Schnitzer and the rest of the makeshift back-three (consisting of Schnitzer’s fellow out-of-position player Yannik Keitel and young Man City loanee Juma Bah) worthy of above average marks in our player ratings piece.

“When you seize your chance, you become more and more integrated into the team,” Schnitzer remarked in the mixed zone.

Augsburg’s defensive personnel shortage left Baum with little choice but to turn to the 18-year-old, who ordinarily operates as a defensive midfielder. Chrislain Matsima, Jeffrey Gouweleeuw, Noahkai Banks, and Calvin Brackelmann were all unavailable, leaving the returning Juma Bah as the only other natural central defender available to the FCA trainer.

“We were missing four central defenders and were still able to field a back-three,” Baum remarked at the post-match press conference. “And I believe we were able to implement something that we always aim for as a club. Namely, to integrate young players in at the right moment. And we witnessed that with Tim Schnitzer, who delivered a fabulous performance in his first game and the team really helped him out with that.”

“A huge compliment to the whole team,” Baum continued near the end of his opening press conference statement. “And let’s single out Tim again. He’s from our own academy and has been with us since the U9s. I think he grew up just a couple of blocks away from the academy. I think we can be very happy about the result, the team, and the next homegrown talent who was able to start today.”

Schnitzer’s first Bundesliga start represents another significant milestone in a development pathway that Get German Football News has followed closely since the summer. The Augsburg-born midfielder made his senior debut against RB Leipzig last March before spending the summer attempting to establish himself as a genuine first-team option. Schnitzer’s versatility emerged as one of his most promising qualities, with the youngster capable of operating as a defensive midfielder, central defender, or No. 8.

Baum had already identified Schnitzer as one of Augsburg’s most promising academy graduates in August, even mentioning the teenager among several FCA players he hoped might eventually attract Germany senior national-team consideration. Our 2026/27 Augsburg tactical preview also highlighted Schnitzer’s encouraging preseason performances, including his goal against Bournemouth, and suggested that the youngster should have opportunities to collect meaningful Bundesliga minutes.

Sunday’s injury-enforced selection certainly provided one such opportunity. Schnitzer responded by completing the entire 90 minutes and helping Augsburg end their seven-match winless streak. Whether the teenager retains his place once Augsburg’s established defenders return remains to be seen. Baum nevertheless has another option at his disposal, and the youngster who once served as a ball boy at the WWK Arena has taken another important step toward becoming a Bundesliga regular.

GGFN | Peter Weis – reporting from the WWK Arena

Amorim: ‘Milan pace did damage against brave Frosinone’

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Ruben Amorim explains how he made the most of ‘brave’ Frosinone’s approach to run riot in the spaces and focus on the ‘pace’ in his Milan side for a 5-1 win. ‘The team believe in the plan.’

It was a morale-boosting performance from the Rossoneri against a Frosinone side who changed tactics to 3-4-3 with a False 9 for this trip to San Siro.

It left massive gaps in defence that Milan took full advantage of with a counter-attacking masterclass, as after Gabriele Calvani’s own goal, Goncalo Ramos, Ardon Jashari, Alphadjo Cisse and Christian Pulisic ran riot.

The only negative was the Patrizio Masini goal to temporarily make it 2-1 going into the half-time break, while Goncalo Ramos went off with a muscular twinge.

“The main thing is that we have a plan in the game, and the team believe in the plan,” Amorim told DAZN Italia.

“You know Frosinone as well as I do, they are all about full pressure, I understood that with a lot of points coming to San Siro, they would be really brave. With the pace we have with Rabiot, Moreira, Pulisic and Ramos, we could make transitions.

“We changed a little bit the way we play the game because of our opponents, and it was a good night for us.”

Goncalo Ramos scored his first goal since October 19, while Jashari hadn’t ever scored in the Milan jersey before this evening.

“We are building principles and understanding the game. We knew Frosinone are about marking man to man, that’s why Pulisic looked like he was in every position, because we knew they would follow us, and we could create a lot of danger with changing roles,” continued Amorim.

“It doesn’t matter who plays, every player in training needs to understand every position on the pitch, so that we can change the characteristics. There are so many things I saw in the game that we need to improve, but we stuck to the plan, and that is something I am pleased with.”

When Ramos went off with a calf twinge, why did Amorim turn to Cisse and not Francesco Camarda?

“You saw the game, we did not spend many minutes around the box, the space was behind. If we were controlling the game in a different way, Camarda was the right way to play, but Cisse is really good with pace. You saw the trouble he made for the opponent was not holding the ball, it was the runs behind, the passes, not a typical striker,” explained the coach.

“I didn’t notice it was Ramos who scored the goal, it was more important that it was 2-0, because Milan need lots of goals to control a game, and also Frosinone had to put more men forward at 2-0, so it opened up more spaces for us.”

Milan have won five of the last six Serie A matches, and consolidated fourth place in the hunt for Champions League qualification.

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The 5-3-1 New York Giants are in the pole position to win the NFC East. They're hoping to maintain their first-place cushion in the division on Sunday, searching for a bounce-back victory after falling to the Washington Commanders on "Thursday Night Football" last week.

Returning to the win column this time out means beating an AFC title contender. The Giants are hosting the 7-2 Jacksonville Jaguars, who enter MetLife Stadium having won six of their past seven games.

Jacksonville is firmly in command of the AFC South. Liam Coen's team is among the most complete in the NFL this season. After taking down the Tennessee Titans last week, the Jaguars were the only team in the league that ranked inside the top 10 in EPA in all three phases of the game, according to ESPN Insights, which also noted that the Jags' plus-125 point differential was the best by any team through its first nine games since the 2021 Buffalo Bills (plus-145).

Jacksonville's defense is imposing. The Jaguars came into the weekend allowing only 16 points per game, the second fewest of any team this time around. Trevor Lawrence is posting a 21:5 touchdown-to-interception ratio. Giants quarterback J.J. McCarthy has his work cut out for him to keep pace and navigate everything Jacksonville throws his way.

Fortunately for McCarthy, he's buoyed by a Giants defense that's giving up the third-fewest passing yards per game (184.1) this season.

Follow along with Yahoo Sports for live updates, highlights and more as the Jaguars face the Giants on Sunday afternoon, airing on CBS.

Despite dealing with knee and ankle injuries, the Jaguars are expected to give running back Bhayshul Tuten the "bulk of carries" in Week 11, per CBS reporter Tracy Wolfson.

Tuten's practice reps have been limited as he works through the injuries, logging limited sessions on Wednesday and Friday while sitting out of Thursday's practice. He was left without any official designation on the final injury report.

Tuten is the team's leading rusher with 609 yards and five touchdowns this season, adding another 206 yards and three scores through the air. Chris Rodriguez is his backup.

Mauricio Pochettino bemoans ‘unfair’ Liverpool incident in ‘painful’ Manchester United defeat

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mauricio Pochettino has called Manchester United’s 4-0 defeat by Liverpool “so painful”.

The Argentine, making his bow as the Red Devils’ latest head coach at Anfield, watched on as his new side were thrashed by Andoni Iraola’s hosts in a one-sided affair.

Leny Yoro was dismissed on eight minutes for denying Alexander Isak a clear goalscoring opportunity, and Dominik Szoboszlai, Florian Wirtz, Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz netted the goals that handed the Reds a huge three points, and deservedly so.

Reflecting on the heavy loss, which demonstrated the sheer size of his task ahead, Pochettino bemoaned the red card as a key turning point at such an early moment in the encounter.

"So painful. So disappointed,” the former Tottenham and Chelsea head coach said.

“It's not ok to start a game in the Premier League with this situation. The game changed completely after [eight] minutes. Now we can explain everything but the damage is done, it's so painful.

"The players are so, so feeling the pain of the result. I told them to be calm. It was an unfair situation that happened. We need to accept that reality. We need to try to increase our focus, concentration and make better decisions on the field.

"We also need to be critical of ourselves but some situations happened in the game that we are not happy with."

The 54-year-old identified two reasons why he believed Yoro should not have been sent off, despite VAR validating referee Sam Barrott’s on-field decision to give the French defender his marching orders.

"The ball was for the goalkeeper,” Pochettino explained.

“In that situation, if you review the VAR you can see for sure, he never had the intention to connect with Isak.

“When the control was long with the striker, the ball was going to the goalkeeper, he runs and goes down.

"In Leny's case, it wasn't intentional. It was an unusual situation that is normal, everyone who plays football can feel it.

Mauricio Pochettino suffers unwanted Premier League first in Man Utd loss vs Liverpool

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Saikat Mandal | 22 Nov 2026 19:24

Mauricio Pochettino's Manchester United reign got off to a disastrous start as the Red Devils suffered a humiliating 4-0 defeat against arch-rivals Liverpool at Anfield in the Premier League on Sunday.

The Argentine took charge of the club earlier this week after Man Utd parted ways with Michael Carrick, leaving him with barely a few days to work with his new players on the training ground.

Pochettino had won just three of his previous 19 games before Sunday's encounter, and the final whistle brought no improvement to that record.

The defeat also had a damaging impact on the league standings, with Man Utd dropping to 19th in the Premier League table on eight points after 11 matches, with only Crystal Palace faring worse.

According to Opta Joe, Pochettino became the first manager in Premier League history to see his team concede twice and receive a red card in the first half of his opening match in charge of a club.

The game was only eight minutes old when the Man Utd backline was caught napping, forcing Leny Yoro into a challenge on Alexander Isak that resulted in a red card.

Liverpool capitalised on United's defensive frailties, scoring twice before the break through Dominik Szoboszlai and Florian Wirtz.

The hosts enjoyed greater control after the interval and added two more goals through Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz, with the former also setting a Premier League record of his own.

Pochettino also entered the Man Utd history books for the wrong reasons, suffering the joint-heaviest defeat by any Red Devils manager in his first game in charge, matching Lal Hilditch's 4-0 loss against Bolton Wanderers in October 1926.

Considering Man Utd's struggles so far this season, the defeat against Liverpool need not spell the end of the world for the Red Devils or their new manager.

Pochettino's immediate priority will be to collect as many points as possible and create some breathing space between his side and the teams at the bottom of the table.

There were precious few positives to take from the performance, aside from Senne Lammens's display, but Pochettino will hope to see greater improvement from his players in the coming days.

Any regrets? Pochettino’s start at Man Utd descends into farce in Anfield rout

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Liverpool fans did not miss a beat. They never do. “Poch is at the wheel,” they goaded as this calamity unfolded. But at the wheel of what, a clown car? The look of thunder on Mauricio Pochettino’s face said it all as he got off to the worst start of any Manchester United manager – or head coach – in 100 years.

The Pochettino bandwagon rolled into town with the usual fanfare of a new era, new belief, new hope; and then the wheels came off, the doors collapsed and the engine exploded. The opening quarter of an hour really was that farcical.

In that time, away to Liverpool of all places, they were 2-0 down, a player down – and down and out. If Pochettino had any uncertainty over the scale of the task ahead of him, there it was laid bare. Any regrets? Whatever the salary, he could be forgiven for having a few as he watched from the touchline.

Maybe Pochettino, too, can take some responsibility. Even this early on. Was it wise to drop Harry Maguire and hand Matthijs de Ligt his first start in 357 days? Not from the way De Ligt was at fault for the first and, arguably, the second goal. Did the head coach start with a back three or a back four? The players seemed confused. It eventually ended up as a back five.

But this was a collective failing for United. From top – with director of football Jason Wilcox caught on camera with his head buried in his coat (he really needs to work on that poker face) – through the club and out on the pitch. Pochettino is big on building a culture. He has to abolish the calamity and the comedy.

The work needed at United is monumental as the inexplicable strategy of rebuilding the attack last year, the midfield in the summer and the defence next year was once again exposed. Whoever did that as a way of building a successful team? Instead United have conceded 34 goals already this season, the most in the top five European leagues.

United will argue, with justification, that Leny Yoro’s eighth-minute sending off was harsh given Alexander Isak appeared to have lost control of the ball. But what was Yoro thinking as he pushed the forward over? And what was De Ligt doing as he watched on, or lumbered on, after failing to track the run?

Leny Yoro is SENT OFF after eight minutes! 😱 https://t.co/VKYvckvBBP

And what were United doing with that shambolic defensive wall, with Bruno Fernandes left waving a leg as Dominik Szoboszlai could not believe his luck at the space he saw? The Liverpool midfielder simply whipped a low free-kick into the corner of the net.

Dominik Szoboszlai SLAMS home the free-kick! 💥 https://t.co/Q09eEi0m0B

The second goal? It highlighted that mismatch as Rio Ngumoha easily beat Diogo Dalot, not for the first and not for the last time, and pulled back a cross that Florian Wirtz hooked in off the crossbar. De Ligt was, quite frankly, all over the place as he again watched on.

It can be argued that, after that disastrous start, Pochettino stemmed the bleeding just as it appeared set to be a bloodbath. But that may also have owed much to a lack of killer instinct from Liverpool, who did not go for the jugular. Still they scored four. It could – should – have been a repeat of the 7-0 from three years ago.

"This is how they dreamt it here!" ❤️ Florian Wirtz doubles Liverpool's lead. https://t.co/RQbA6OXRLA

Into the second-half and the chant from the Kop was clear. “Attack, attack, attack,” rang out as they demanded more goals; more humiliation. The wastefulness was highlighted by Bradley Barcola running the ball out of play when through and Isak slicing a shot with only Senne Lammens to beat.

A third goal did come with Barcola easily beating Lisandro Martínez, using his pace down the right, before crossing low for the outstanding Ngumoha to sweep home. “Going down, going down,” sang the Liverpool fans before adding: “You’re not famous anymore”. Not famous but certainly infamous.

Before the end even the United loyalists began to stream out of the away end, missing a fourth goal as – aptly – possession was needlessly lost and substitute Víctor Muñoz exchanged passes with Wirtz before steering his shot around Lammens.

Liverpool bulldoze the 10-man visitors to earn an emphatic 4-0 victory. Pochettino’s first game in charge of Man Utd ends in a heavy and disappointing defeat. Bruno Fernandes says he has shelved talks over a new contract because it is not the “right moment” for discussions over his future as Manchester United captain.

Fernandes, 32, is out of contract next summer although United have a 12-month option to extend his deal to 2028.

The Portugal midfielder is understood to have rejected two contract offers from United since the summer but said all his focus needs to be on trying to help the team out of their current plight.

United head into Sunday’s game against Liverpool at Anfield second from bottom in the Premier League.

“We spoke about the contract at the end of last season,” Fernandes told Sky Sports.

“We came in at the beginning of this season speaking about the contract, but about the context of the team and the club, I just put that to a side because I don’t think it’s the right moment, neither for me, nor for the club, to be talking about negotiations.

“The most important thing now is that the club gets back to winning games.”

Fernandes said claims he wants more money or a longer-term deal on offer are incorrect and that if finances were the issue he would have left Old Trafford before now. The captain has previously rejected lucrative offers from Saudi Arabia.

“The club offered me a new contract, so I think that, from their side, shows that they want me to stay,” he said.

“There’s no negotiations ongoing, but I’ve seen people saying that ‘He’s asked for more money, he’s asked for more years’. If I wanted more money, I wouldn’t be here.

“I had somewhere to go to get much more money than being at Manchester United. I could have been in a place that is much warmer than Manchester.

“Two summers ago, three summers ago, last summer, I always had the chance to leave and I always chose to be here.

“The thing is, I’ve always showed how much I care about the club and how much I want to win at this club. And I always said, in the moment the club thinks it’s time for me to move on, I will move on.

“I’m not going to be the player that I’ve seen many in this time at Manchester United. They’re going to stay here just by staying, just finish my contract and then we will see. In the moment the club says, ‘Bruno, it’s time to part ways’, I’m going to be the first one walking out the door.”

United chief executive Omar Berrada said of Fernandes on Friday that it was “true that we’re having conversations about his new contract” but that the club preferred “to keep that private”.

Having been voted PFA Player of the Year last season, Fernandes’ form has dropped off this term like that of the team.

He was dropped for the 3-0 defeat by Bournemouth in the wake of a 4-1 drubbing by Como when he criticised the tactics and approach of then head coach Michael Carrick.

Mauricio Pochettino, Carrick’s successor, confirmed on Friday that Fernandes would remain as captain.

Fernandes said the United fans had been “far too patient with us” and hopes Pochettino - who wanted to bring the Portuguese to Spurs in 2019 - will bring a fresh energy.

“He’s someone that likes to bring a very good energy to the team, someone very positive, very strong, strict, said it straight away that he won’t allow us to not give him what he demands from the team, from each player,” Fernandes said.

“He wants the club to succeed so much, he wants to win games, and that’s pretty clear and obvious by the way he and his staff speak and want us to improve.

“Obviously [in 2019] I was a very young player that was eager to come to the Premier League. They had [Harry] Kane there, they had Dele Alli, Son, the chance of playing with those players in front was very attractive.”

Liverpool 4-0 Man Utd: VAR Expert Casts Verdict on Leny Yoro’s Red Card

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Leny Yoro's sending off in Manchester United's 4-0 defeat to Liverpool came in controversial fashion, and VAR expert Dale Johnson has weighed in on whether referee Sam Barrott got the decision right.

Regardless of the red card, this was a pitiful performance from United and the worst possible start for Mauricio Pochettino. He watched his side capitulate within eight minutes when Matthijs de Ligt, strangely handed a start, got caught out and Yoro raced back before barging into Alexander Isak.

Yoro sent Isak to the turf just outside the box, and Barrott deemed this to have been a red card offence due to the French defender being the last man. Most United fans aren't using the dismissal as an excuse for their team's fifth league defeat of the season, but many are questioning whether the challenge warranted such punishment.

Isak was played through on goal and the Liverpool striker took a heavy touch, sending the ball a fair distance away from him when Yoro made the challenge. VAR didn't feel the need to intervene and potentially downgrade the red card to a caution.

Johnson delved into Barrott's decision and explained that the referee had four key considerations to make over Yoro denying an obvious goalscoring opportunity. These are "the distance between the offence and the goal", "the general direction of play", "the likelihood of keeping or gaining control of the ball" and "the location and number of defenders and attackers".

Three of those factors were satisfied because Isak ran directly towards goal in a central position and United had no covering defender. The main question is the "likelihood of keeping or gaining control of the ball."

United will argue that Yoro's contact came after Isak's touch and therefore there is no case that the challenge caused the player to over-run the ball. Johnson suggested in his BBC Sport article that Barrott and VAR John Brooks had to assess whether Isak would definitely have got to the ball ahead of goalkeeper Senne Lammens.

Johnson added: "And that if he had, it would have been a good scoring chance. If there is any doubt, then it should not be a red card. It is a borderline case, and as such the VAR would not be expected to intervene for a clear and obvious error."

United were put to the sword thereafter when Dominik Szoboszlai scored the opener with the resulting free-kick (10'). Florian Wirtz added a second (13') before Rio Ngumoha (66') and Victor Munoz (83') sealed an emphatic victory for Andoni Iraola's men.

There were differing opinions on the red card amongst Sky Sports pundits as United legends Gary Neville and Roy Keane disagreed. Neville defended Barrott's decision and tore into Yoro for a "clumsy" challenge and "poor anticipation".

Keane, however, felt Yoro could feel hard done by. He was "amazed" to see De Ligt fail to read the flight of the ball, arguing that Isak's heavy touch meant he wasn't going to regain possession:

"Isak has done well, but I actually don't think he's going to get the ball... He's not getting the ball. There's no way he's getting the ball. It's clumsy from United, it's one straight ball over the top. You shouldn't be in so much trouble, but there's no way he's getting that ball. Absolutely not."

Manchester United legend was less than impressed after the final whistle against Liverpool

This is the second time this season Yoro has been sent off, compounding a miserable afternoon for United. Their loss to Liverpool leaves them sitting 19th after 11 games played, and they will be without the Frenchman for Brentford's visit to Old Trafford on Saturday unless they successfully appeal the decision.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.
